- Proof: `cargo test -p cypcb-cli --test the_routed_board_grades_the_same_twice` -> 2 passed; before the change the first read `route says 4 violations about the board it wrote and check says 5`. `cargo test --release -p cypcb-autoroute --test benchmark_validation -- --ignored` -> 2 passed.
- DONE: **three of the six routing benchmark boards were not KiCad files, and KiCad said so on the first try.** `Failed to load board: Expecting '(' in 'stm32_breakout.kicad_pcb', line 81, offset 3`, and the same at multi_ic line 137 and led_blink line 151. Line 81 is `; -- U1: STM32F103C8T6 (LQFP-48) --`: the files carry Lisp-style comments, 19 in stm32_breakout, 20 in multi_ic, 1 in led_blink, and KiCad's s-expression reader has no notion of a comment - it expects `(` and stops. This project's reader tolerates them, which is the whole reason six fixtures claiming `(generator "pcbnew") (generator_version "8.0.0")` have carried this since 2026-03-14 with nothing able to say so. shift_driver, qfp_fanout and plane_board have no comments and always loaded.
- Only the comment lines were removed: 40 deletions, no other byte touched. KiCad then loads all three and reports 16, 168 and 81 violations - the same counts it gives for KiCad-converted copies of the same boards, so it is reading the same geometry. Every benchmark figure is unchanged to the digit: led_blink 2/0, stm32_breakout 187/99, multi_ic 304/200, shift_driver 65/34, qfp_fanout 318/149, plane_board 28/13.
- **Measured and deliberately not taken: converting the fixtures to genuine KiCad output.** `kicad-cli pcb upgrade` re-saves all six in KiCad's current format with 51 to 539 uuids apiece, and the importer reads the result as the same board on every figure it reports - component count, net count, segments, vias, board size, layers and zones identical for all six. The router disagrees. Swapped in, `benchmark_all_fixtures_drc` fails: multi_ic 357 violations against a ratchet of 356 and 255 shorts against 243, qfp_fanout 195 shorts against 191, and plane_board 30 against 28 with 15 shorts against 13 - on a board whose measured noise band is zero. The ratsnest moves too: stm32_breakout routes 899 connections before and 967 after, multi_ic 945 and 1032. KiCad's round trip normalises pad geometry in a way the metadata counts cannot see.
- NEXT-ACTION: **genuine fixtures are a re-baseline, not a cleanup, and should be taken as one.** Every ratchet, every band in `via_price_sweep`, and every table in `docs/routing.md` is measured against these boards; converting them means re-measuring all of it in one commit and saying so. Worth doing - the boards would then be what they claim to be - but it is a session, not a fire.
- NEXT-ACTION: **repair has to start from the board the first pass started from.** The blockers are the good idea - they are real DRC output, not a heuristic - but an attempt must route the whole ratsnest with those cells forbidden, not the remainder of a board that is already wired. That means clearing the autorouted copper before each attempt, and re-measuring whether the pass pays at all once it is comparing like with like.
- DONE: removed the rip-up retry loop in `astar_improved.rs` that could never run twice (every path returned or broke on the first pass - the author had already worked this out in a comment and silenced the lint instead of deleting the loop). Took the dead `max_iterations` parameter out of two functions with it. Behavior unchanged, `clippy::never_loop` unblocked.
- DONE: all 3 failing integration tests fixed by one defect. PathFinder's rip-up called `mark_route(cell, u32::MAX)` before `clear_route(net_id)` - that marks the cell occupied and erases its owner in the same step, so the clear found nothing and every ripped-up route left a permanent wall. `RoutingGrid::clear_cells` clears the recorded cells directly, which is also `cells.len()` work instead of a full `width * height * layers` scan per rip-up. blink.cypcb: `Partial { unrouted_count: 5 }`, 23 segments, 21.0mm, score 5036.0, 123.07s -> `Complete`, 45 segments, 6 vias, 182.5mm, score 212.5, 84.40s. Integration suite 432.26s -> 86.75s, 5 passed 0 failed.
- **This defect was fixed once already.** M005-SUMMARY describes it exactly, quotes the same post-fix numbers (45 segments, 6 vias, 182.5mm) and claims a proof test `test_blink_led_zero_unrouted`. Neither the fix nor that test exists in the published history - `git log --all -S` points at `db11eb0 chore: clean repo for public release`. Treat every M005 "validated" claim as unverified until re-run.
- DONE: the CI regression gate no longer rewards giving up. It asserted `route_count > 0` plus quality thresholds, and every quality metric improves when the router abandons connections - so it passed on 1 unrouted / 7 routes and failed as soon as the router completed the board. Now asserts 0 unrouted and >= 20 routes first, with quality thresholds recalibrated against a complete solution. Verified in both directions: passes on HEAD, fails on `ded9461` with "1 unrouted connections, threshold 0".
- DONE: R107 from 13 violations to 3. `tests/drc_report.rs` (new, `--ignored`) printed every violation with kind and coordinates instead of one number, and all 13 were `clearance` - mostly a trace against the pad it connects to, at 0.00mm. Cause: `apply_routes` spawned bare `Trace`/`Via` entities, while DRC's same-net exemption queries for a `NetId` *component*. The renderer already spawned `(trace, net_id)`; KNOWLEDGE.md K012 states the rule. Same routing before and after (0 unrouted, 22 routes, 79.61mm): 13 violations / composite 13542.6 -> 3 violations / composite 3542.6. Ratchets tightened to the measured values.
- DONE: classified the remaining 3. They are **real inter-net violations**, not grid artifacts - `R1 ↔ trace 'GND'` at 0.07mm (needs 0.15), `trace 'SW_OUT' ↔ trace 'GND'` at 0.00mm and `trace 'SW_OUT' ↔ via 'GND'` at 0.00mm. `.gsd/PROJECT.md` claimed the opposite and has been corrected.
- **Failed experiment, tried twice, do not try a third time:** hard-blocking any cell within `clearance_cells` of foreign copper during path expansion. The geometry it targets is real - routes are marked cell by cell while a trace is twice a cell wide, so two nets in neighbouring cells overlap - but blocking is the wrong instrument. First attempt: 3 -> 4 violations, 22 -> 20 routes, composite 3542.6 -> 4043.6. Second attempt, after the congestion fixes made PathFinder converge properly: identical outcome, 4 violations and 19 routes. A hard block forbids legal geometry too (a trace may run at exactly the minimum clearance), so it costs routes without buying correctness. If this is attacked again it needs a **soft cost** in the neighbour cost function, or a post-route repair pass driven by actual DRC output - not a veto during expansion.
- DONE: **the router obeys a width the design states.** `net VCC [width 0.4mm]` was parsed, carried onto the board model by M1, and then ignored: `paths_to_output` took the width from `rules.constraints_for_net()`, and `PresetRuleSet` returns the same constraints whatever net it is asked about, so a designer's explicit instruction lost to the fab default on every trace. The stated width now wins, floored at the preset minimum - a design cannot ask for copper thinner than the board house will etch. Proven on a two-net board: VCC comes out at 0.4mm on every segment and the net that says nothing keeps the 0.127mm preset. The benchmark fixtures are KiCad imports and declare nothing, so 0/167/110 is unchanged.
- **That NEXT-ACTION was stale, and the check that would have caught it was one command.** The three violations on led_blink are gone - `drc_report` now prints `led_blink.kicad_pcb - Complete, 23 routes, 0 violations`, and `smoother_integration_led_blink` passes its `drc_violations == 0` assertion. The hypothesis written down for them was also already false: `RoutingGrid::from_world` bloats obstacles by `min_clearance + min_trace_width / 2`, half-width included, with a comment explaining why. Read the code before writing the next action from memory.
- DONE: **the benchmark DRC numbers now say who caused them.** The fixtures are KiCad boards with parts already placed, so a violation in the routed board might be one the router made or one the import brought with it, and nothing distinguished the two. `drc_report` runs DRC twice - once on the fixture as imported, once after routing - and lists only what appears in the second run. Measured: led_blink 0 before / 0 after, stm32_breakout **3 before / 176 after / 173 introduced**, multi_ic **5 before / 127 after / 122 introduced**. The router is responsible for essentially all of them; blaming the fixtures would have been comfortable and wrong.
- ~~NEXT-ACTION: refine the clearance narrow phase to actual pads and add a courtyard rule~~ **Done.** `ClearanceRule::component_pads` measures per-pad copper with per-pad nets, `CourtyardClearanceRule` keeps placement collisions reported, and both are registered - `crates/cypcb-drc/src/rules/courtyard_clearance.rs` exists and `lib.rs` boxes it. This line stood after the work landed, which is how a fresh session picks up an action that is already finished.
- DONE: **a probe that says which half of the router to blame.** `tests/grid_vs_checker.rs` (`--ignored`) takes every clearance violation the router introduces, maps its coordinate onto the grid the router used, and reports what that cell was marked as - a route through a cell marked `CELL_PAD` means the search ignores its own obstacles, a violation on a free cell means the marking never happened. Cross-tabbed against the shape of the pair, stm32_breakout's 103: **trace-via 66 (64 of them on free cells)**, trace-trace 33 (18 on pad cells), via-via 3, part-via 1. multi_ic's 28: trace-trace 23 (20 on pad cells), trace-via 3, part 2. Grids are 296x256 at 0.254mm and 197x158 at 0.508mm per cell.
- **Failed experiment, measured, do not repeat: hard-owning the via ring in the grid.** The finding above has an obvious reading - a via's copper is never written into the grid at all, only into the congestion map, where it is a price a later net can pay rather than a fact it must respect. Marking the ring as the net's own copper (`mark_route` over `via_footprint_cells`) made both boards worse: stm32_breakout **124 -> 154** introduced (clearance 103 -> 125, hole-to-hole 3 -> 9, routes 858 -> 965), multi_ic **42 -> 55** (clearance 28 -> 38). Reverted, and the revert re-measured back to 124 / 42 exactly. This is the third time a hard block has cost more than it bought. The reason is arithmetic: the via keepout is `drill/2 + annular + clearance + width/2` = 0.15 + 0.127 + 0.127 + 0.0635 = 0.4675mm, which at 0.254mm per cell rounds up to a 2-cell disc - 0.508mm, nearly double the ring's actual 0.277mm of copper. At this resolution the grid cannot express via clearance without over-blocking. The honest routes are a finer grid near vias, or a post-route repair driven by real DRC output.
- **The repair pass currently buys nothing, and the tracker said otherwise.** `route_board` runs `strategy.route` and then `repair_routes`; the benchmark diagnostics called `strategy.route` directly, so they measured a router the product never ships alone. Pointed at `route_board` they report **exactly the same numbers** - 858 routes / 124 introduced and 1027 / 42, identical to the un-repaired path on all three fixtures. Repair therefore accepts no attempt on any of them today. The 176 -> 167 and 127 -> 110 recorded below were measured before the clearance rule was fixed, and no longer describe this code.
- ~~QUEUED: the integration suite still spends 86s in one test~~ **Stale by a factor of four.** Measured 2026-08-08 across the whole workspace: the slowest suite is `every_hole_is_drilled_in_one_pass` at 21.73s, and the total of every suite is 43.8s. A* is still disabled in the UI, which is D5 and the owner's call.

### V3 - Build, CI and quality gates
- DONE: **a fresh machine can now run the gate, and a test keeps that true.** What the gate and the wasm build need had never been written anywhere runnable: chromium's system libraries were installed into this container by hand, so the next recreate would have taken stage 6 with it; `wasm-opt` became required the day the wasm build stopped going through wasm-pack; and `wasm-bindgen` has to match the version `Cargo.lock` resolves or the module it writes will not load in a browser. `scripts/setup-dev.sh` does all five - toolchain and wasm32 target, the pinned `wasm-bindgen` CLI, binaryen, the viewer's npm dependencies, chromium with its apt half - and every step checks before it installs. Run for real in this container, as root: `[OK] wasm-bindgen 0.2.108 matches Cargo.lock`, `[OK] wasm-opt version 108`, `[OK] chromium installed`.
- `viewer/src/__tests__/the-setup-script-installs-what-the-build-needs.test.ts` reads `command -v` out of `viewer/build-wasm.sh` and requires the setup script to name each tool, checks the playwright and npm steps the gate depends on, requires the `wasm-bindgen` version to come from `Cargo.lock`, and requires the script to be executable. Mutation-checked: renaming `wasm-opt` to `wasm-opti` in the setup script fails it with `expected [ 'wasm-opt' ] to deeply equal []`. **The first version of that check used `includes`, which passes on exactly that typo** - a guard that cannot tell `wasm-opt` from `wasm-opti` is a guard that passes on the mistake it exists for.
- DONE: **the language server did not compile, and nothing in the repository could have told anybody.** `cypcb-lsp` had `default = []` with `server` behind a feature flag, so `cargo build`, `cargo test` and all eight gate stages compiled the crate with `backend.rs` cfg'd out - 2,984 lines that no command touched. Asking for it directly: `cargo build -p cypcb-lsp --features server` -> `error[E0195]: lifetime parameters or bounds on method 'initialize' do not match the trait declaration`, ten of them, `error: could not compile cypcb-lsp (lib) due to 10 previous errors`. One `#[async_trait::async_trait]` against a `tower-lsp` that takes native async methods. Deleting that line builds it; the `async-trait` dependency came out of `Cargo.toml` with it, and `Cargo.lock` is one line shorter.
- **`server` is a default feature now**, because the flag is what let the rot happen and the cost of carrying it is 3.92s: `cargo clean -p cypcb-lsp` then `cargo build -p cypcb-lsp` -> `Finished dev profile in 3.92s`. No new crates resolve - `tokio`, `tower-lsp` and `dashmap` were already in the lock.
- DONE: **the server is driven the way an editor drives it, and it answered with two defects.** `crates/cypcb-lsp/tests/the_language_server_answers.rs` spawns the binary and speaks `Content-Length`-framed JSON-RPC over its stdin and stdout: `initialize` (name and the three advertised capabilities), `didOpen` on a board with `20furlongs` in it (a diagnostic, on the line the bad unit is on), `didOpen` on a board that parses, and `textDocument/hover` over a refdes (the answer names `R2` and its value `1k`).
- **Every design rule violation arrived at line 0, character 0.** `DrcViolation::source_span` is `None` in every constructor in `cypcb-drc` - it is a field nothing fills - so a file with twenty parts got twenty squiggles stacked on its first character, none of them where the part was written. The board model already knows: `sync_ast_to_world` spawns components and traces with a `SourceSpan`, so the diagnostic reads the span off the entity the violation names. Measured before: `"message": "Unconnected pin: R2.2", "range": {"start": {"line": 0, "character": 0}}`.
- **And every one of them was an error.** A part exists before its net does, so `unconnected-pin` and `unrouted-pin` are what a board being written *is*; the editor painted the whole file red from the first keystroke. Those two are warnings now, everything else stays an error. Both fixes mutation-checked: severity forced back to `"error"` fails `a_board_being_written_is_not_an_error`, the span lookup forced to `None` fails `a_diagnostic_points_at_the_part_it_names` with the 0,0 range quoted above.
- Proof: `cargo test -p cypcb-lsp` -> 5 passed; workspace `cargo test --workspace --exclude cypcb-desktop` -> **119 suites, 1463 passed, 0 failed** (116 suites before); `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --workspace --exclude cypcb-desktop --all-targets -- -D warnings` both clean - the newly compiled code cost two `redundant_closure` fixes that no lint run had ever seen.
- **The desktop crate below is now the only workspace member nothing compiles**, and this one says what the pattern costs: a feature flag or an exclusion that hides a crate from the build hides it from the compiler too, and the crate stops compiling without anybody being told.
- DONE: **completion and go-to-definition were asked their first question, and completion answered with the wrong list at the exact moment it is wanted.** `textDocument/definition` from `R1.2` inside a net block lands on the line `component R1` is declared on, and completion inside a closed footprint string offers the built-in library - both already worked, both now proven by protocol. Completion on a line still being typed did not: `component R3 resistor "` with the string open is unparseable, so the AST held no such component, `find_completion_context` read the cursor as "after the last definition" and returned the **top-level keywords** - the editor offered `version`, `board`, `net`, `module`, `assert` inside a footprint string, where accepting one pastes a whole snippet into the string.
- **The cause is that the context came from the AST alone, and a half-written definition is exactly what a parser cannot return.** The context now falls back to the text under the cursor, and only when the AST placed the cursor nowhere: an odd number of quotes on the line means the cursor is inside a string, and `component` as the line's first word makes it a footprint name; otherwise the innermost unclosed `{` is found - skipping braces inside strings - and its opening line names the block. `component`/`board`/`net`/`footprint` bodies offer their own properties.
- Both halves mutation-checked: with the fallback returning `None`, `completion_works_on_the_half_typed_line_it_is_asked_about` and `completion_inside_a_block_nobody_has_closed_yet_offers_its_properties` fail, the second with `["version", "board", "component", ...]` where `value` and `at` belong. The two that passed first time were checked too - `goto_definition` forced to `None` fails the definition test, `footprint_completions` forced empty fails both footprint tests - because a test that has never been red proves nothing.
- Proof: `cargo test -p cypcb-lsp` -> 42 unit, **9 protocol tests**, 0 failed; workspace `cargo test --workspace --exclude cypcb-desktop` -> 119 suites, **1467 passed, 0 failed** (1463 before); `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --workspace --exclude cypcb-desktop --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean. The full gate was not re-run: this fire touches one crate's source and no script, bundle or build setting, and the previous commit's gate run covers the rest.
- DONE: **the server has a manual, and a test holds the manual to the server.** `docs/language-server.md` gives the build command, what the server answers, what it does not, how diagnostics arrive, and a Neovim snippet - marked as written from the protocol rather than executed, because there is no Neovim in this container. What the page claims came from asking the running binary, not from reading the source: `hoverProvider`, `completionProvider` with trigger characters `.`, space and `"`, `definitionProvider`, and `textDocumentSync` with full text on change and on save. `serverInfo` is `cypcb-lsp` `0.1.0-beta`.
- `crates/cypcb-lsp/tests/the_manual_matches_the_server.rs` starts the binary, sends `initialize` and compares both directions: everything the page claims has to be advertised, everything it lists as missing has to be absent, and **anything the server advertises that the page does not mention fails** - the direction that catches the next capability nobody writes down. Mutation-checked: swapping `definitionProvider` for `referencesProvider` in the page gives `the manual promises what the server does not offer: ["referencesProvider"]` and `the server advertises these and docs/language-server.md does not: ["definitionProvider"]`; moving `hoverProvider` into the "does not answer" list gives `these are advertised and the manual says they are not: ["hoverProvider"]`.
- **`docs/architecture.md` claimed a feature that has never existed.** "Semantic tokens (syntax highlighting)" is listed under this crate's key features and the word `semantic` appears nowhere in its 2,984 lines; the browser's highlighting is Monaco's own grammar. Corrected, along with the `server` feature note that still called it optional and blamed proc-macro loading, and the line calling go-to-definition a future enhancement when it works and has a test. The README's single "LSP Server" link pointed at `docs/api/lsp-server.md`, which is about the WASM bridge and not about this binary at all - now two entries that say which is which.
- Proof: `cargo test -p cypcb-lsp --test the_manual_matches_the_server` -> 4 passed; `cargo test -p cypcb-lsp` -> 42 unit, 9 protocol, 4 manual; workspace -> **120 suites, 1471 passed, 0 failed**; the documented build command run as written: `cargo build -p cypcb-lsp --release` produces a 1.94MB `target/release/cypcb-lsp` that starts.
- **The second flake did not reproduce, and the honest report is that rather than a fix.** After the leaked dev servers were killed, the Playwright stage was run three times on its own - `CI=1 npx playwright test --reporter=line` -> **102 passed** at 53.9s, 53.8s and 52.9s - and `ps aux | grep -E "[v]ite|[p]laywright|[c]hromium" | wc -l` reads **0** before and after each. `ps aux | grep -c "[c]ypcb watch"` is 0 too, so the new watch test does not leak its child either. The most probable cause is the 39 leaked `tsx server.ts` processes the previous fire removed - they held ports and machine load while the browsers ran - but that is a sequence, not a mechanism, and it is written here as one. The stage names its failing specs now, so the next occurrence identifies itself instead of needing this search again.
- **The heartbeat's own background check was reporting a four-day-old failure.** `/config/bench/full-test.log` was last written 2026-08-05 and ended `error: 2 targets failed` for `benchmark_validation` and `smoother_integration`; both have passed on HEAD every time they were re-run since. A stale log that says "red" costs a re-verification every fire and teaches the next reader to ignore it. Rewritten from inside the container: `cargo test --workspace --exclude cypcb-desktop` -> `exit=0`, **148 `test result: ok` lines and 0 `FAILED`**, timestamped 2026-08-09.
- NEXT-ACTION: none in this vector that a fire can close. A VS Code extension is the only thing left between this server and a user who does not read documentation, and shipping one is a packaging decision - marketplace identity, versioning, release channel - rather than a fire.
- DONE: gate stages 1 and 2 are green for the first time. `cargo fmt --check` was failing on 30 files (rustfmt version drift, not authored noise) - reformatted the workspace. `cargo clippy --workspace --exclude cypcb-cli --exclude cypcb-desktop -- -D warnings` was failing on 11 deny-level errors across 5 crates, including a tautological assertion (`comp.value.is_some() || true`) that made a parser test unable to fail. Proof: both commands exit 0; touched crates re-tested, 11 result lines all ok.
- DONE: `scripts/quality-gate.sh` no longer excludes `cypcb-cli` from the clippy and test stages. That exclusion is why `cypcb check` could ship as a no-op for months. Removing it surfaced one lint (`&PathBuf` parameter that wants `&Path`); with that fixed the gate covers the CLI. Proof: `cargo clippy --workspace --exclude cypcb-desktop -- -D warnings` -> Finished; `cargo test -p cypcb-cli` -> 7 + 12 passed.
- DONE: ran the JavaScript-side stages for the first time and cleared two. Stage 4 (eslint) had 31 errors - 26 unused symbols, mostly dead functions and the imports feeding them, now deleted; 5 never-reassigned bindings. Stage 8 (jscpd) had one clone: `fetch3DModel` repeated the body of `fetch3DModelByUuid`. Both green, `tsc --noEmit` clean too.
- DONE: stage 5 (vitest) green, 149 of 149. Two stale-value bugs, not test bugs. Layer colours lived in three tables and the settings defaults - the ones feeding the Preferences colour pickers - carried an older palette, so the dialog showed five colours the board never draws; `render-config.ts` and `settings.ts` now both read `LAYER_COLORS` and the test asserts against it. The routing tests expected angle snap off while the code deliberately follows KiCad and starts it on.
- DONE: stage 6 starts. Vite was dying on `viewer/svg-pcb` and `viewer/circuitron` - reference checkouts of other projects, committed as submodule pointers with no `.gitmodules` entry, so a fresh clone gets empty directories and `git submodule update` fails. Untracked and ignored, Vite's scan pinned to this app. Then chromium needed `libnspr4.so`; installed with `npx playwright install-deps chromium` inside the container (container filesystem, not the image - a recreate needs it again). Also untracked 42MB of `viewer/test-results`.
- DONE: triaged the Playwright failures instead of fixing them one by one. The largest cause is not a bug: **the entire Route UI is hidden in `index.html`** behind `<span class="toolbar-anchor" style="display:none">` with the comment "Autorouter disabled - needs fundamental rewrite". A DOM probe against the running app confirmed `.tb-route-group` measures 0x0, which is why every `#route-btn` click timed out, and `#tuning-toggle` does not exist at all. `tuning-panel` and `variant-panel` now skip with that reason written above the describe. Also confirmed `#theme-toggle` is `display: none` by design, so the `ui-architecture` expectation of it is stale.
- **Gate status, measured 2026-08-05:** 1 fmt PASS, 2 clippy PASS, 3 cargo test PASS, 4 eslint PASS, 5 vitest PASS, 6 playwright **66 passed / 31 failed / 14 skipped** (was 68/43/0, and 0 attempted before the web server was fixed), 7 autorouter benchmark PASS, 8 jscpd PASS.
- DONE: `project-manager.spec.ts` from 8 failures to 2, plus `ui-architecture` fixed. A DOM probe against the running app showed the specs, not the app, were behind: 5 template cards ship (4 templates plus blank) where the tests expected 4 and `templateCount` 3; the recent-files section hides itself when empty instead of rendering a "No recent files" message; the import button reads "Import File" since "Open" was reserved for the workspace list; display names strip the `.cypcb` extension. Every one of those is in CLAUDE.md - the tests predate it.
- DONE: the editor cluster was one app bug wearing two masks. `ensureEditorReady()` guarded on a boolean set only after its await, so the toolbar click and the idle preload both ran `initEditor()` on the same container - two Monaco editors, which is exactly the `.monaco-editor` strict-mode violation, plus a race over the panel's visibility. On top of that the preload undid an "auto-show" by testing for a `hidden` class the container never carries, so the editor opened by itself a few seconds after every load. Memoized the init promise, dropped the bogus undo. `editor.spec.ts` fully green.
- DONE: `Ctrl+Shift+T` never toggled the theme. Rewriting two specs off the hidden `#theme-toggle` onto the documented shortcut exposed it: the handler matched `e.key === 'T'` only, and the browser reports `t` or `T` depending on layout and input path. Every other letter shortcut in `main.ts` already accepted both. The old specs could not have caught it - one asserted only that `data-theme` was still light or dark, which holds when nothing happens.
- DONE: the JLCPCB search cluster. The app scored the whole query as one string, so "0805 10k" - a package and a value, never in one field - matched nothing; terms are scored separately now, and attributes are read from the older `extra` shape the scorer had been ignoring. The specs were measuring through a mock that had drifted from the API: no CORS header on cross-origin fulfillments (browser dropped every response), one fixture answered for all 34 category endpoints (panel hit its 20-result limit), LCSC codes pinned to positions, and a debounce assertion of "exactly 1 request" from when a search hit one endpoint.
- DONE: the last two unblocked failures were both real defects. With ten or more recent files the project manager's template cards sat at `top: -205` with `scrollTop: 0` - `align-items: center` on a scrolling container puts the top of taller-than-viewport content outside the scrollable area, so the templates were unreachable by a user, not just by a test; `margin: auto` on the child fixes it. And the E2E mocks only intercepted `easyeda.com`, while the dev build talks to the Vite proxy paths, so those requests were leaving the machine for real on every run.
- **Gate status, measured 2026-08-06: all eight stages pass.** `./scripts/quality-gate.sh` -> `=== All stages passed ===`. 1 fmt, 2 clippy, 3 cargo test, 4 eslint, 5 vitest, 6 playwright **94 passed / 0 failed / 17 skipped**, 7 autorouter benchmark, 8 jscpd (0 clones). This is the first run in which stages 7 and 8 executed at all: the three `benchmark-screenshots` tests failed on D5's hidden Route button, the script exits on the first failing stage, and so the autorouter ratchets and the duplication check had never been reached. They are skipped now with the reason written above the describe, the way `tuning-panel` and `variant-panel` already were - a gate that is permanently red is a gate nobody reads, and it was hiding two stages behind one owner decision.
- DONE: **the manufacturing files had no test of their own, and now have one that provably fails.** Everything checked so far stops at the model; the Gerbers and the drill file are the last thing between a design and a wrong board, and nothing asserted a routed trace reaches the layer it belongs to. Measured first on real output - `examples/blink.routed.cypcb` gives 14 trace blocks and 4 vias, 62 draw commands on F_Cu and 20 on B_Cu - then pinned as `crates/cypcb-export/tests/routed_board_reaches_the_files.rs`: a two-layer net joined by one via, built by hand so the test fails for export reasons only. It asserts each layer carries its own copper, that neither carries the other's (which would be a short through the board), and that the via becomes exactly one hole at the right coordinate with the right tool. Mutation-checked rather than assumed: `if trace.layer != layer` forced to `if false` and the via loop given `.take(0)` turn both tests red, restored with `git checkout`. Workspace after: 1311 passed, 0 failed.
- ~~NEXT-ACTION: next fires should move to V1's empty DRC checkers~~ **Stale: there are none.** `silk_clearance.rs` was the last stub and it was implemented two vectors ago; all 18 rules in `crates/cypcb-drc/src/rules/` do work. The three `benchmark-screenshots` still need the Route UI back (D5), which is the only thing blocked here.
- DONE: **`cypcb parse -o json` prints the board model now, which is what it has always said it prints.** Both arms of the match printed the AST - the `json` arm with a comment blaming a cargo problem that no longer exists, since `cypcb-cli` has depended on `cypcb-world` for a long time - so the two formats returned identical bytes and the documented one was a lie. The model is built the same three steps `check` takes (resolve imports, sync into the world, read the world), so the two commands cannot disagree about what a file says, and it carries what only the model knows: whether a component's footprint was found, which net each pin ended on, the nets' constraints, the traces, the vias and the zones. Sorted by refdes and net id, because ECS iteration order is not spawn order and an unsorted dump makes every diff noise.
- Proof: `crates/cypcb-cli/tests/parse_prints_the_board_not_the_ast.rs`, five tests - every example prints the six model keys and no `definitions`; the two formats differ on the same file; `v2-imports.cypcb` reports six components all with `footprint_known` and seven nets, none of which the AST can show; `pour-island.cypcb`'s plane arrives with its net and a real outline; and the same file twice prints the same bytes.
- DONE: **the drift between the two parsers is measured, and it is not small.** `viewer/src/__tests__/parser-drift.test.ts` walks every board in `examples/` through both implementations and diffs the model. Three defects, all user-visible:
  - **`import` does nothing in the browser.** `v2-imports.cypcb` draws an empty board where the CLI sees six parts on seven nets.
  - **Modules are not instantiated in the browser.** `v2-modules.cypcb` draws the two parts written inside a module body as though they were top level, under their local names, and finds one net; the CLI instantiates and gets seven parts with prefixed names on five nets. `v2-interfaces.cypcb` is the mirror image - the viewer draws two parts and two nets from a body that is never instantiated, where the CLI correctly draws nothing.
  - **A pin-to-pin trace is invisible in the browser.** `trace VCC { from R1.1 to C1.1 }` with no explicit geometry is copper in the model and in the Gerber, and nothing on screen: four of the examples are affected.
- **The plain subset agrees, which is the other half of the measurement.** Twelve of the nineteen examples - a board, parts, nets, explicit trace geometry, zones - match on every field checked, so this is a gap in what the TypeScript reader implements rather than a difference in how it reads what it does.
- The test pins the list rather than tolerating it: it fails when a new disagreement appears **and** when one of these is fixed without being struck off. `npx vitest run` -> 21 files, 178 passed.
- DONE: **the pin-to-pin trace was worse than the drift said, and the defect was in Rust.** Chasing the viewer's missing trace led to `get_pin_position` in `cypcb-world/src/sync.rs`, which returned the **component's own position** under a comment calling it "a good approximation" and a four-line list of what a complete version would do. So `trace VCC { from R1.1 to C1.1 }` was copper between two part centres, touching neither pad it names - and that copper is what the exporter writes into the Gerber. On `examples/syntax.cypcb` the segment ran 5mm to 20mm, the two part origins; it now runs **4.5mm to 19.5mm**, which is R1 pad 1 to C1 pad 1.
- The endpoint is the pad the reference names, offset by the footprint and turned by the part's rotation - the same transform `copper.rs` and `RoutingGrid::populate_pads` use, so the checker, the router and the writer agree on where a pad is. A footprint nobody registered or a pin it does not have falls back to the part's position rather than dropping the copper, because a missing pin is reported where the netlist is checked and a silently missing trace is the harder failure to notice.
- Proof: `crates/cypcb-world/tests/a_pin_to_pin_trace_lands_on_the_pads.rs`, three tests that compute the expected point from the library rather than repeating a number - the copper starts and ends on the named pads; a part turned 90 degrees moves its pad off the x axis and the copper follows; and a pin the footprint does not have still draws something. Workspace 1419 passed, clippy clean, viewer 178 passed.
- DONE: **the viewer draws pin-to-pin traces now, and it draws the same copper the CLI does.** `parseSource` read the `trace` block and produced no segment unless it carried a `path`, so `trace VCC { from R1.1 to C1.1 }` was invisible on screen while the exporter wrote it into the Gerber. It resolves both pads with the same transform the engine uses - the footprint's offset, turned by the part's rotation, added to the part's position - and resolves them **after** the whole file is read, so a trace may name a part written below it.
- **The check was strengthened before the entries were struck off, which is the part worth keeping.** `parser-drift.test.ts` compared trace *counts*; it compares the geometry now - net, layer, width and every endpoint in nanometres, with one spelling for a layer because the two sides print `TopCopper` and `Top`. Under that stricter comparison the four boards agree exactly, so the fix is a match rather than a coincidence of counts. `KNOWN_DRIFT` is down to the three module and import boards.
- Proof: `npx vitest run` -> 21 files, **178 passed**, with the drift test green only because the list shrank; `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` on both changed files silent.
- DONE: **V4's parser decision is costed and taken: route (b), a Rust reader.** `docs/one-parser.md` holds the measurement. Route (a) - a wasm C toolchain - has to build our generated `parser.c` **and the tree-sitter runtime, 13,647 lines of C across 13 files**, with clang against a wasm sysroot, while the engine targets `wasm32-unknown-unknown`, which has no libc; the sysroot that exists is `wasi-libc`, for `wasm32-wasip1`. And it buys nothing beyond parsing: the duplicate TypeScript reader would still be there, so modules and imports would still land twice, as the pin-to-pin trace just did.
- **The number that decided it: nothing consumes the concrete syntax tree.** Every caller - `cypcb-cli`'s five commands, `cypcb-lsp` (`document.rs` calls `parse()`), `cypcb-render`, `cypcb-world::sync` - takes the AST. So a reader that fills `ast.rs`'s 47 public types with correct spans serves the tooling unchanged, `imports.rs` and `errors.rs` are untouched, `parser.rs`'s 3,226 lines of CST-to-AST are **replaced rather than added to**, and `parseSource`'s 439 lines are deleted.
- The oracle is already in the repo, which is what makes this a project rather than a rewrite: `cypcb parse -o ast` prints today's AST as JSON, so a new reader is correct when it prints byte-identical JSON for every example. Order of work is in the doc, four steps, each shippable alone.
- DONE: **step 1 of the one-parser plan: a tokenizer and a reader in Rust, and it builds for wasm.** `crates/cypcb-parser/src/lexer.rs` (tokens: words, numbers, strings, punctuation, both comment forms) and `src/reader.rs` (recursive descent for `version`, `board`, `component`, `net`, `trace`), behind a `rust-parser` feature. Nothing in the shipping path calls it. The claim that matters is measured: `cargo build --release -p cypcb-parser --no-default-features --features rust-parser --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` **finishes**, where the tree-sitter path cannot without a wasm C toolchain.
- **It is checked against the parser it will replace, not against hand-written expectations.** `tests/differential.rs` reads every example both ways and compares the ASTs as JSON with spans stripped - tree-sitter's node boundaries and a hand-written reader's are not the same bytes, and pinning them now would pin tree-sitter's shape rather than the language's meaning. **80 definitions across 9 boards, identical.** The ten boards it does not claim are listed with the construct that skips them, and a test asserts each of those files exists, so the list stays honest as the reader grows.
- **Two guards, because two readers that both return nothing agree perfectly**: the test counts the definitions it compared and fails under 40, and the comparison was mutation-checked - making the reader ignore `locked` turns it red, and putting it back turns it green.
- The gate runs it: stage 3 of `scripts/quality-gate.sh` now runs the feature's differential test explicitly, since the plain workspace run does not build the feature. Workspace 1419 passed, clippy clean.
- DONE: **step 2 in one pass: the Rust reader covers every v1 construct.** `footprint` with its pads, courtyard and both silk forms; `zone` and `keepout`; `netclass` with the same constraint block a net carries; `outline`. The differential test went **80 definitions across 9 boards to 113 across 13**, all identical to what tree-sitter produces. `four-layer.cypcb` came off the skip list without any work - it was listed for a stackup it does not have, which is what a skip list costs when it is written from a guess rather than a run.
- What is left is the v2 four - `module`, `import`, `interface`, `assert` - plus the two boards written to fail parsing, where the question is error parity rather than coverage. Wasm still builds: `cargo build --release -p cypcb-parser --no-default-features --features rust-parser --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` -> `Finished`.
- DONE: **the Rust reader reads the whole language.** The v2 four landed together because they interlock: `module` with a body that holds the same definitions a design does, `use X as Y at ... rotate ... { PIN = net }`, `import` in both spellings, `interface`, and `assert` with comparisons, `within`, and all three tolerance forms. Typed values came with them - `value 10kohm` fills `typed_value` **and** the text, which is what the tree-sitter parser does and what anything that only prints a value needs.
- The lexer grew the operators the assertions need, longest-first so `>=` never reads as `>` then `=`.
- **The differential test now covers every example except the two written to fail parsing: 147 definitions across 17 boards, identical.** Mutation-checked on a v2 path: making the module instance accept a port without its `=` turns it red, putting it back turns it green. Wasm still builds; workspace 1419 passed; clippy clean.
- DONE: **step 1b, error parity - and measuring it found a real hole.** The reader skipped any word it did not recognise at the top level, which was correct while it was incomplete and wrong the moment it covered the language: `frobnicate 3` parsed clean, where tree-sitter reported it. A typo in a keyword meant a part quietly missing from the board and nothing said so. Unknown words are an error now, with the word in the message.
- `tests/error_parity.rs` pins both directions: five broken sources the language rejects and the reader must reject too, three good ones it must not reject, the repo's own two demonstration files, and the position - the reader's first error has to land within twenty bytes of tree-sitter's on the same fault, which it does. **What is not pinned is the count or the wording**: tree-sitter reports every token it could not place, this reader reports the first thing it wanted and walks to the next definition, so `unknown_keyword.cypcb` is two errors against one. Pinning those equal would pin a recovery strategy rather than a language.
- The gate runs it beside the differential test. Workspace 1419 passed, clippy clean.
- DONE: **step 3(a): the browser engine parses `.cypcb` itself, and the thing that stopped it was a feature line that was not true.** `cypcb-world`'s `sync` feature declared `cypcb-parser/tree-sitter-parser`, but `sync.rs` only ever uses `cypcb_parser::ast`, which every build of that crate has. That false dependency is what kept the AST-to-board path out of the browser. `sync = []` now, and `cypcb-render`'s `wasm` feature carries the Rust reader plus sync, so `PcbEngine::load_source` is exported to JS for the first time.
- **Measured, both columns**, on `viewer/build-wasm.sh` output: raw **751,995 -> 1,044,164 bytes**, gzipped **290,004 -> 411,147**. The artifact is clean - its 50 imports all come from the wasm-bindgen glue and **none from `env`**, which is what proves no C got linked in.
- Proof that the reader is really the one running: `crates/cypcb-render/tests/the_browser_build_reads_the_language.rs` builds with the browser's own features and asserts a board comes back with its parts, that **a module instance places both of its parts under the instance's name** - the exact defect the TypeScript reader has - and that `frobnicate 3` is still reported.
- DONE: **step 3(d), the half that matters: the browser reads `.cypcb` with the engine.** `WasmPcbEngineAdapter.load_source` called `parseSource` - the TypeScript reader - and shipped the result into the engine as a snapshot. It calls `wasmEngine.load_source(source)` now and reads the board back, so components, nets, traces, vias, zones, the copper the pours became, the ratsnest and the DRC violations all come from Rust. The trace-replay loop went with it: there is nothing left to hand back to an engine that already holds the model.
- **Verified where it counts, in a browser**: `npx playwright test` -> **93 passed, 0 failed, 17 skipped**, including the boards that load, render, toggle to 3D and report geometry. `npx tsc --noEmit` silent, `npx vitest run` 21 files and 178 passed.
- DONE: **the second reader is deleted.** `parseSource` and the helpers only it used - `parseUnit`, `getFootprintPads`, `getFootprintSilk`, `normalizePinName` - are gone, `viewer/src/wasm.ts` is 1,804 lines down to 1,210, and `MockPcbEngine.load_source` refuses with a sentence instead of parsing: a board that looks right and is not is worse than a build that says it has no engine. `zone-parsing.test.ts` and `parser-drift.test.ts` went with it - both existed to measure a reader that no longer exists, and what they covered is asserted in Rust.
- **Measured, and it is not a size win**: the wasm module went 751,995 -> 1,044,164 bytes (290,004 -> 411,147 gzipped) and all the JavaScript in `viewer/dist` went 14,791,848 -> 14,781,791. 292KB of wasm against 10KB of JavaScript. What it buys is that a board using `module` or `import` now draws what the CLI exports, which is the defect the whole vector started from.
- Verified in a browser after the deletion: `npx playwright test` -> **93 passed, 0 failed, 17 skipped**. `npx vitest run` -> 19 files, 172 passed. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint src/wasm.ts` silent.
- DONE: **the native half of step 3: `parse` is the Rust reader everywhere, and no default build compiles C.** `rust-parser` is `cypcb-parser`'s default; the tree-sitter parser is `tree_sitter_parse` behind its own feature. The CLI's five commands lost `CypcbParser`, `cypcb-lsp` and `cypcb-render`'s native feature stopped asking for the C parser, and `imports.rs` moved to whichever reader the build has, since it only ever worked on the AST.
- **The proof is the absence**: `cargo tree --workspace -i tree-sitter` -> `package ID specification 'tree-sitter' did not match any packages`. The crate is not in the graph, so `build.rs` never runs `cc` and the fresh-clone story stops needing a C toolchain.
- The two comparison tests still need both parsers in one binary, so they build with `--features tree-sitter-parser` and the gate names them: `cargo test -p cypcb-parser --features tree-sitter-parser` -> **115 passed**, differential 147 definitions across 17 boards unchanged. The default workspace run is 1357 passed - lower than the 1434 before the flip because the tree-sitter parser's own unit tests only build with its feature, which is where they belong.
- The crate's own documentation was rewritten with it: its Quick Start built `CypcbParser` and its low-level section drove `tree_sitter::Parser`, both of which stopped existing by default and failed as doctests.
- DONE: **spans measured, and the risk was not there.** `tests/spans_point_at_the_source.rs` checks them against the thing that cannot drift - the source text: an identifier's span has to spell the identifier, a string's span has to be the quoted literal, a definition's span has to start with its keyword and contain everything inside it. All three hold on every example. Mutation-checked: moving an identifier's span one byte turns the first test red.
- **And the two parsers agree exactly.** The diagnostic in the same file compares definition spans between them: **147 identical, 0 different, widest gap 0 bytes.** The differential test strips spans by choice, not because they differ, and the LSP's hover and goto are reading the same offsets they always did.
- DONE: **measured where the router's cost model is blind, and the obvious answer is wrong.** `grid_vs_checker`, re-run today: the largest group of introduced clearance faults on both dense fixtures is a route's copper against a part's pad **on a cell the grid marked as a pad** - 109 of stm32_breakout's 206 mapped faults, 112 of multi_ic's 215. The obvious cause is the pad zone opening every pad a net has rather than the two a connection runs between.
- **Failed experiment, measured, reverted: opening only the connection's own two pads.** stm32_breakout 239 -> 299 after and 136 -> 175 shorts, multi_ic 336 -> 398 and 166 -> 219, with **nothing left unrouted** on either. So the router is not reaching through pads it has no business near; it is inside its own pad's disc, colliding with a **neighbouring part's** pad that the disc happens to cover. Eighth restriction tried in this vector, eighth to lose. Recorded in `docs/routing.md` with the reading it refutes.
- DONE: **a crate that only built inside the workspace now builds on its own.** The workspace declared `cypcb-parser = { ..., default-features = false }`, written when the default feature was the C parser and a wasm build had to opt out. With the reader as the default it means *no parser*, and `cargo clippy -p cypcb-autoroute --all-targets` failed on `cannot find function parse in crate cypcb_parser` while the whole-workspace run passed - feature unification was hiding it. The override is gone from the workspace and from `cypcb-drc`, `cypcb-world` and `cypcb-render`, and each of six crates now lints clean alone.
- DONE: **the radius was the suspect, and it pays.** The pad zone's radius had been the pad's own copper plus a flat three cells since the first version, under a comment reading "generous but safe" - 0.762mm on the 0.254mm grid, wider than the gap between the two pads of an 0402. Swept: **two cells beats three on both dense boards and both columns** - stm32_breakout 239 -> 216 violations and **136 -> 86 shorts**, multi_ic 336 -> 290 and 166 -> 131, all outside the noise bands this vector measured. Zero is not the floor: with no margin stm32_breakout leaves a connection unrouted and takes seven times as long.
- **It ships as a variant, not as the default, because led_blink goes the other way** - two near misses become one short, which is backwards under a ranking that puts shorts first. `PathFinder Tight Pads` is the eighth variant, and **stm32_breakout picks it in best-of-eight at 216 / 86** where its previous pick `Low-Via` gave 216 / 114. multi_ic keeps `Pad Aware`, led_blink keeps `High-Density`. The default path and the gate are untouched at 2/0, 239/136, 336/166.
- DONE: **named the fault that keeps the narrower opening out of the defaults, and refuted two fixes for it.** At margin 2 led_blink's new short is `D1 <-> via 'GND': 0.00mm` - a via whose ring lands on a part's pad, not a trace crossing one. Raising `pad_layer_change_penalty` (now a config knob rather than a constant) does not touch it: the short survives every price to 1000, and stm32_breakout gives back its win at 150 (216 -> 263). So the search is not choosing to pay - it never sees the fault.
- **And that is the finding worth keeping: `foreign_cells_in_via_keepout` counts `net_at` only.** That map holds routed copper; a pad's net lives in `pad_net`, because a rip-up clears `net_map` and a pad is not ripped up. **A via pays for landing its ring on another net's trace and nothing for landing it on another net's pad.** Measured directly: counting those cells at the shipped price of 0.25 takes stm32_breakout 239 -> 259 and multi_ic 336 -> 392 with 166 -> 216 shorts, because the keepout disc covers many pad cells on a dense board. Reverted - the blind spot is real, the price for it is not that one.
- DONE: **the blind spot is closed, the mechanism is confirmed, and it still does not earn a place in the defaults.** Pads inside a via's keepout are counted apart from routed copper now and priced by `via_foreign_pad_penalty`. At the narrower opening it does exactly what the theory said: led_blink's via-on-a-pad short **disappears at every price from 0.02 up**, taking that board from 2 violations with 1 short to **1 with 0** - better than the shipped default. The dense boards do not follow: stm32_breakout goes 216 / 86 to 272 / 147 at 0.02 and 239 / 124 at 0.05, multi_ic 290 / 131 to 277 / 121 at 0.02 and 330 / 168 at 0.05.
- **So it ships at zero.** No opening and price together beat what each board already picks in best-of-eight - stm32_breakout 216 / 86 from `Tight Pads`, multi_ic 267 / 106 from `Pad Aware`, led_blink 1 / 0 from `High-Density`. The knob is there, the numbers are in `docs/routing.md`, and what is missing is a board that wants it.
- DONE: **the command line stopped shipping the router's second-best answer.** `cypcb route --in-house` routed once with the default settings; `--variants` - best of eight, which the viewer has always done - was a flag nobody would know to pass. Measured on `examples/blink.cypcb`: one run gives **9 violations with 6 shorts**, best-of-eight gives **5 with 3**, for 0.9s against 0.06s. Best-of-eight is what `--in-house` does now, and `--fast` is there for when the wait matters more than the board.
- **And the coverage test that was supposed to catch a variant going missing did not check the last two.** `default_variant_configs_cover_the_measured_settings` asserts a variant prices via rings and guards pad keepouts; it now also asserts one prices a foreign pad (multi_ic picks it at 267/106) and one narrows the pad opening (stm32_breakout picks it at 216/86). A list nobody checks is a list a refactor quietly shortens.
- - NEXT-ACTION: none in this vector worth its cost. Eleven instruments have now been measured on these three fixtures; the two that pay are in the defaults, three are variants a board picks, and the rest are in the dropped table with the numbers that killed them. **The next real gain is a cost model rather than another knob**, and the evidence for that is this fire: every knob left moves one board and gives it back on another. Next fires should go elsewhere - V1's CLI surface and V5's roadmap features are both untouched for a dozen fires.
- ~~QUEUED: put the browser system deps in the image or a CI setup step~~ **Done as a script in the repository**, which is the half that does not need an infrastructure decision - see the entry at the top of this vector. ~~The specs audit is separate and still open.~~ **Done** - the entry below.
- DONE: **the flake had a name, and then a cause: 39 leftover dev servers.** Two gate runs had failed and passed on their own; the stage learned to print names last fire, and this time it did - `the-dev-server-guards-the-disk`, at `refuses to read a file outside it` with **`expected 'the private key' to be undefined`**. The guard had not broken: the test was talking to somebody else's server. `ps` found **39 `tsx server.ts` processes** left over from earlier runs, because the test signalled `npx` and the `tsx` child under it lived on, and the next run's guessed port - random, but from a range those strays were sitting in - bound nothing and connected to one of them.
- Two fixes, both of which end the class rather than the instance: the server prints the port it really bound and the test reads it from that output with `CYPCB_WS_PORT=0`, so it can only ever talk to the server it started; and the child is spawned `detached` and killed by process group. Three full suite runs green afterwards with **zero** leftovers, against 39 before.
- DONE: **the README's routing table had gone stale in every row and listed half the boards.** It said led_blink 0 -> 2, stm32_breakout 12 -> 271, multi_ic 60 -> 210; measured now: 2, 180 and 291, with shift_driver 65, qfp_fanout 309 and plane_board 28 missing from the table entirely. Replaced by the command that prints all six with their ratchets, per this project's own rule that a number which moves without a schema change ships as the query. The same treatment for "sixteen rules", which is 20 today - `grep -c 'Box::new(rules::'` answers it.
- Three feature rows were behind the code: KiCad export is done (`cypcb to-kicad`), differential pairs are declared and checked rather than planned, and the parts engine is partly there - a design names the part, the viewer fetches its footprint, the BOM carries it.
- DONE: **`docs/architecture.md` claimed two calculations the code has never had**, and now carries the reachability measurement instead of leaving it to be repeated. The `cypcb-calc` entry listed "Microstrip/stripline impedance" and "Thermal resistance (junction to ambient)"; the crate is `trace_width.rs` and a 24-line `lib.rs`, and the word `impedance` appears once, in a comment calling it future work. Both entries now say how much of the crate anything can reach: one entry point of `cypcb-calc`, one module of four in `cypcb-platform`.
- **And the document is guarded rather than re-read.** It was fixed once by hand - two crates were missing entirely, every size snapshot had drifted - which is a fix with a shelf life. Three tests: every crate has a section, no section describes a crate that is not there, and **every dependency the document claims between workspace crates is really in that crate's `Cargo.toml`**. Mutation-checked: adding `cypcb-router` to `cypcb-calc`'s dependency line fails with `cypcb-calc does not depend on cypcb-router`.
- DONE: **measured the other two crates D3 asks about, and one of them was printing a number nobody could explain.** `cypcb-calc` is live - the checker, the language server and the engine all call it - but only one of its entry points: `min_width_for_current(current, is_external)`, which takes neither the copper thickness nor the temperature rise that IPC-2221 needs to answer at all. So `trace 'VBUS' is 0.300mm wide for 3.0A: IPC-2221 wants 1.367mm` was true of 1oz copper and a 10C rise, and said neither. The rule reads the fab's copper weight now - a field the presets have always carried and nothing read - and the message ends `at 1.0oz copper and a 10C rise`.
- **No board's verdict moved**, and that is asserted rather than argued: every preset states 1.0oz, which is what the calculator's default was. A third test sets 2oz by hand to show the thickness changes the answer, which is why it belongs in the message.
- `cypcb-platform` is the other half of D3 and the measurement is now precise: `src-tauri` is its only dependant and uses one of its four modules - `Menu`, `MenuBar`, `MenuItem` in `menu.rs`. The file, storage and dialog modules, about 850 of its 1,300 lines, have no caller anywhere, and the crate that would use them is the one nothing in this container compiles. That is the whole of what the owner has to decide about it.
- DONE: **the last orphan crate has a caller, so D3's watcher half is closed by fact rather than by decision.** `cypcb-watcher` was 184 lines and three passing tests that nothing in the workspace used - written for hot reload, while the hot reload that shipped is chokidar in `viewer/server.ts`. `cypcb watch <file>` is its first caller: it checks the design, then checks it again on every save, and re-checks when a file the design imports changes too. The browser has had hot reload since the dev server was written; a terminal had nothing.
- **One save is not one event, and measuring that was the whole fix.** The debouncer forwards a save as a stream of notifications - one every 200ms for as long as the command runs - so the first working version ran **24 checks for a single edit**, and 91 for two. Draining the queue did not help, because the events keep arriving rather than sitting in it. The command remembers what each file held when it last checked it, and a file whose bytes have not moved is not a save whatever the operating system says: **one edit, one check**, measured at 24 -> 27 -> 30 violations across two appends.
- **The README's own example did not hold.** It said a file `check --preset pcbway` calls 28 violations is 28 to `score` too; measured, that file is 24 to `check` and 12 to `score`. Not a disagreement about the rules - `score` routes the board before measuring it, so it reports its own routing. On the same routed file both say 27. Corrected with both numbers and the reason. The preset list there was stale too: eight, against the eleven the merged registry now answers to.
- **Two gate stages have failed inside a full run and passed on their own** - playwright once, vitest once - and neither left a name behind. Both stages now print the failing specs on the way out. A flake nobody can name is a flake nobody can fix.
- DONE: **the editor did not know a third of its own language.** Monaco colours the language from a keyword list in `editor/cypcb-language.ts`, and it had fallen behind the grammar by three whole top-level constructs - `outline`, `netclass` and `diffpair` - plus `use`, and the properties `lcsc`, `path`, `silk`, `point` and `implements`. A keyword written in plain text beside coloured ones tells a reader it is not part of the language, which is the same lie in a different place as a construct missing from the guide.
- `viewer/src/__tests__/the-editor-knows-every-keyword.test.ts` reads the members of `_definition` out of the generated grammar, the same source `the_guide_shows_every_construct` uses, and fails when the editor's list falls behind. Mutation-checked: with `diffpair` taken out it reports `the editor writes these in plain text: expected [ 'diffpair' ] to deeply equal []`.
- DONE: **the files that build and serve the project were checked by nothing.** `tsconfig.json` included `src/**/*` and the gate linted `src/ e2e/`, so `server.ts`, `vite.config.ts`, `vitest.config.ts` and `playwright.config.ts` had never been type-checked or linted - and `server.ts` is the file that reads and writes a developer's disk on request from a web page, the one whose path-traversal holes this project closed two fires ago. Both now cover them, and the first run found eleven lint problems and four type errors.
- In `server.ts`, which is ours, they were real: `message: any` on the WebSocket dispatcher became a `ClientMessage` shape, and three `catch (err: any)` reading `err.message` off anything at all became `catch (err)` through a `reason()` that narrows. The type checker then caught the save handler taking `{file: string, content: string}` while the dispatcher hands it optional fields - it validates them itself, so it takes what it is sent now.
- In the config files the `any`s describe somebody else's untyped surface, so they are allowed there and an error in `server.ts`. Two type errors were third-party mismatches worth writing down rather than hiding: `router` is an http-proxy option Vite passes through and does not type, and `mergeConfig` wants the object shape while Vite's `defineConfig` returns every shape a config may take.
- **And the gate now says which spec failed.** Stage 6 ended in a bare `✗ playwright` while the names scrolled past in a hundred lines of progress output - a flake seen twice still had no name to chase. It prints the failing specs on the way out.
- DONE: **audited all 101 Playwright tests for assertions that cannot fail, and four of them could not.** The Rust side of this audit was done weeks ago and found a missing DRC rule; the browser side was still open. Every spec was read for the three shapes that pass on a dead feature - a tautology (`>= 0` on a count), an assertion nested in an `if` that guards the very thing being tested, and a body that never executes. Twenty files, sixteen tests flagged by shape, four real:
- **`net highlight activates when clicking a trace` has never executed one line of what it is named after.** Its whole body sat behind `if (traceCount > 0)`, and it loaded `blink.cypcb` - nine components, seven nets and **no traces at all**, an unrouted board. Inside that dead branch was a second `if`, and inside that a click on the centre of the canvas with the comment "Not asserting non-null because center-click may not hit a trace in headless" - the assertion had been removed on purpose. It loads `uat-routing-locked.cypcb` now, converts a real segment's midpoint through the viewport `main.ts` publishes for exactly this, clicks it, and requires the highlighted net to be the one it clicked, the trace to be selected, and Escape to clear it.
- **`DRC violations show error badge` passed when no violation was reported.** `if (badgeVisible)` checked the count and opened the panel; the `else` checked that the status bar did not say "WASM Error", which is true of a checker that finds nothing - the one failure the test is named after. The badge is now required, and so is the word `clearance` in the panel. That second assertion is the one that matters: moving the two parts from 0.5mm to 25mm apart **still shows the badge** (unconnected pins are violations too), so badge visibility alone never proved a clearance check ran.
- **`debug surface reports valid geometry counts` asserted `>= 0` on four counters**, which is true of a 3D scene that built nothing - the characteristic failure of a 3D view. Each counter is now compared against the same snapshot the 2D side draws, on a board with copper on it rather than on blink, where a segment count is 0 against 0.
- **`Ctrl+Shift+T toggles theme` asserted that `#theme-icon` had text in it**, which it does before the key is pressed. The icon is one of three characters, one per setting, so it now has to change and to match the theme actually stored.
- All four mutation-checked against the code they test, not against themselves: with `highlightedNet = trace.net_name` deleted, `clicked the midpoint of a segment of net VCC and nothing was highlighted`; with `updateThemeIcon` returning one icon for every theme, `the icon did not change, so the shortcut did nothing`; with `buildTraces` handed an empty array, the segment counts disagree. Proof: the four specs green at **25 passed**, the three mutants **3 failed / 18 passed**, and each failure is the intended test.
- The other twelve flagged tests are legitimate and were left alone: guards inside `page.evaluate` that only shape the value returned, `test.skip(condition)` runtime guards in `one-checker.spec.ts`, and the three suites that are `describe.skip` and named in `.gsd/REQUIREMENTS.md`'s coverage-gaps table.
- ~~QUEUED: eslint does not see `viewer/e2e/`~~ **Done, and the rules it brought found a feature with no code path.** Stage 4 ran `eslint src/`, so 3,100 lines of test code had never been read by a linter. It reads `src/ e2e/` now, and the specs are held to `eslint-plugin-playwright`'s recommended set plus two rules chosen because this project shipped the defects they catch: `no-conditional-expect` (an assertion inside an `if` that guards the thing under test) and `no-conditional-in-test`. `any` stays allowed, as in `src/` - it is how a spec reaches `window.__pcbEngine`.
- Before: **121 errors** (120 `no-explicit-any`, 1 unused variable). With the Playwright rules on and `any` allowed: **10 problems** - 12 conditional expects in `variant-panel.spec.ts`, 4 conditionals in test bodies, 3 `not.toBeVisible()`, an unused variable, and two assertions that read a value instead of awaiting the locator. All fixed; `npx eslint src/ e2e/` is silent and `npx tsc --noEmit` is clean.
- Two rules are **off with a number rather than a shrug**, both recorded here as debt: `no-wait-for-timeout` (**63 sites** - a canvas has no locator to await, so most are waiting for a render loop to settle and each wants a `waitForFunction` on a condition) and `prefer-locator` (**87 sites** of `page.click('#id')` - locators retry, a raw click does not).
- **Rewriting `variant-panel.spec.ts` to drop its twelve conditional expects turned up why it could never have passed.** The suite is skipped because the Route button is hidden (D5), so the assertions were rewritten to be unconditional and then run for real with the wrapper unhidden. Two things came out. Its fixture board declares `component R1 0402 "0402"`, which is not a component kind - `cypcb check` on the same text says `Missing a footprint name in quotes` twice and exits 1, and the browser loaded it as **0 components, 1 net** and routed nothing. And with that fixed to `resistor`, the board routes (`[Routing] Routed 1 segments in 0s`, 1 trace in the snapshot) while `__variantPanel` stays `{visible: false, variantCount: 0}`.
- **`showVariants()` has no caller.** `grep -rn showVariants src e2e` returns its own definition in `src/variant-panel.ts` and nothing else; `main.ts` imports `initVariantPanel`, `hideVariants` and `isVariantPanelVisible`, and routes through `auto_route_with_params`, a single run. The engine's `auto_route_variants()` exists and `src/wasm.ts` declares it. So the panel, its rows, its CSS and its hover-preview callbacks are unreachable from the UI, and **unhiding the button is necessary and not sufficient** - recorded in `.gsd/REQUIREMENTS.md`'s coverage-gaps table, which used to say only "D5". The suite stays skipped and now states what the panel must do the day somebody wires it.
- The spec also clicked `#tuning-toggle`, which does not exist in `index.html`; the opener is `#route-menu-btn`. Same shape as the `#share-btn` finding above.
- **And the setup script left the machine unusable for the next person.** The apt steps want root, so the whole script tends to be run as root, and npm's cache ends up owned by root - after which `npm install` as the development user stops with `Your cache folder contains root-owned files`. Hit for real this fire installing the lint plugin. `scripts/setup-dev.sh` hands the cache and `node_modules` back to the owner of the checkout when it finishes as root.
- DONE: **the screen could not say which layer a trace is on.** Colour is how a PCB tool answers that, and this one coloured every trace by its net - not as a setting but as `const colorByNet = true` in three places with nothing able to pass false, so the `top_copper` and `bottom_copper` colours `getTraceColor` defines were unreachable for traces. Found by being asked the question with both tools open: pcbnew says F.Cu red and B.Cu blue, this said seven colours for seven nets. View > Colour traces by net switches between the two readings and remembers the choice; the default is unchanged, because net colours are worth having too.
- Proof: `npx vitest run src/__tests__/a-trace-can-say-which-layer-it-is-on.test.ts` -> 3 passed, the third being the one that matters: two traces on different layers carrying the same net match by net and differ by layer, so neither reading has collapsed into the other.
- DONE: **the Import File button did nothing for a KiCad board, and three separate silences hid it.** Reported by the owner on a real board of his own - 400x80mm, 33 parts, 76 nets, 395 trace segments - which loads by drag-and-drop and did nothing at all through the button.
- **Two code paths load a file and only one learned to read a KiCad board.** Drag-and-drop takes `ext === 'cypcb' || ext === 'kicad_pcb'`; the button's path took `cypcb` and `ses` and dropped everything else into `Unknown file type: .${ext}` written to a status bar the project manager overlay is covering at that exact moment. So a whole file type being unsupported looked identical to a button that does nothing. The button now reads a KiCad board, and an unknown type is said out loud rather than written where nobody can see it.
- **`saveToStorage` swallowed every failure under a comment reading "silently degrade".** A board is stored with its source and a thumbnail - over half a megabyte for this one - and when the write is refused the settings in memory and the settings on disk part company with nothing said. It now reports what it could not write and how big it was, and `storageError()` lets a caller tell the user.
- **A project card that cannot open its board said so only to the console.** `onLoadRecent` was handed every stored source and gave all of them to the DSL reader, so a `.kicad_pcb` reopened from its own card fed `(kicad_pcb ...)` to a parser that has never seen an s-expression; it now reads the first token and picks the right one. A card with no stored source now says "Not stored - open the file again" on the card itself.
- **`onRefreshThumbnail` overwrote a good entry with nothing.** It runs every time the project manager opens and wrote `source: null` whenever there was no source in hand, which is the state after a load that did not finish - so one bad load made a card permanently unopenable. It now refuses to write over what is stored and says why.
- Proof, in Firefox rather than headless Chromium, because that is where the fault lived: `#pm-open-btn` clicked, the real file chooser handed that board -> the status bar named it as loaded, project manager hidden, no `Unknown file type`. Seeded with the owner's exact broken state - that board listed with `source: null` - the dead card names itself, the import then works, and the source comes back stored at 482815 bytes.
- **Owner instruction, 2026-08-11: every exception must be handled.** Handled means recovered from with a stated fallback, or surfaced - never swallowed. The viewer has 16 `catch {}` blocks across six files; the ones fixed here were the ones that cost a user their afternoon, and the rest are worth reading against that rule.
- ~~NEXT-ACTION: **read all sixteen swallowed catches against "recovered or surfaced".**~~ Read - thirteen were already right, three were not. See above. `src/jlcpcb.ts` has eight, `src/main.ts` four, and one each in `imports.ts`, `routing.ts`, `settings.ts` and `wasm.ts`. Most return a sensible fallback; what matters is whether the user is ever told when one fires.

- **OPEN, reported by the owner while wiring a board by hand: a trace that was already drawn disappears when the next one is drawn.** Seen twice, cause not established. It is not the one being drawn that goes - it is the previous one, which is the shape of a fault in the round trip rather than in the routing.
- Where it has to be looked for, because every interactive trace makes this journey: `engine.add_trace` puts it in the world, `syncTracesToEditor` replaces the whole managed section of the editor with `engine.export_traces_as_dsl()`, the editor's own 300ms debounce then fires `loadDesign(content)` - which **clears the world and parses the text again**. So a trace exists in copper only for as long as the DSL writer can spell it. Anything the writer cannot express is gone on the next keystroke, and nothing says so: `dsl.rs` already documents one such gap, a via's outer diameter, which is rebuilt from a default rather than read.
- ~~NEXT-ACTION, and it is worth a session of its own: **make the round trip countable, then break it on purpose.**~~ Done - both halves are counted, see V5. What is left is breaking it on purpose. Count the traces in the engine before `syncTracesToEditor` and after the reload that follows; a difference is a loud error naming the net, not a silent loss. Then drive the interactive router from the end-to-end suite - two traces, three traces, a trace on each layer, one with a via, one whose segments do not chain, one on a net whose name needs quoting - and see which of them does not survive being written down and read back. The owner's report is two occurrences with no pattern yet; the instrument is what turns that into a cause.

- **Owner request, 2026-08-11: neck-down, and a way to reach it that is not typing.** Raised from the same session: on a mains board `netclass Mains [current 10A]` gives a trace several millimetres wide, correctly, and a 2.54mm pad pitch has nowhere to put it. Arriving at a pad, the copper reaches the neighbouring pad and DRC fires - which is the checker being right and the design having no way to say what every other EDA lets you say: that the last few millimetres before a pad may be narrower, because a short length of thin copper does not have time to heat.
- Three levels, and only the first exists today:
  1. **Two `trace` blocks, one wide and one narrow.** Works now, says nothing about intent, and the checker cannot tell a deliberate neck from a mistake.
  2. **`neck 0.8mm for 4mm` inside a trace** - the width and the length it is allowed to run at it, so the rule can be checked rather than trusted.
  3. **`netclass Mains [current 10A neck 0.8mm within 5mm of pad]`** - declared once, applied at every pad approach, and DRC reports it as a stated neck rather than as a violation.
- DRC needs a fourth thing with it: a violation category that means "narrower than the current asks for, and said so" rather than a clearance failure. Otherwise level 3 trades one wrong report for another.
- **And it must be clickable.** The owner's words: a Sims-style menu - right-click a trace or a pad and get the choices on the spot, neck, width, layer, delete, rather than going to the text for something you are doing with the mouse. The language stays the record; the menu is how you reach it while routing. `interaction.ts` already carries the routing keys - F flip layer, / posture, Q corner mode, A angle snap - so the actions exist and have no surface.
- NEXT-ACTION: **level 2 first**, because it is the smallest thing that makes a necked trace legal and checkable, and everything above it needs somewhere to write the result. The radial menu comes after, since it is a way of reaching syntax that has to exist first.

- **OPEN, found on the owner's board and not explained: the viewer draws copper on F.Cu that KiCad does not.** Rendered side by side - `kicad-cli pcb export svg --layers F.Cu,B.Cu,Edge.Cuts` against the viewer's own canvas on the same file - the bottom copper matches shape for shape, including the eight 3.048mm mains zigzags. The top copper does not: the viewer shows a fan of long straight diagonals radiating from the left of the board that KiCad's plot has no trace of. Turning the ratsnest off changes nothing, so they are being drawn as copper rather than as unrouted connections. Not the autorouter - nothing is routed on load, and the Route button is hidden. Either the importer assigns some geometry to the top copper layer that does not belong there, or the renderer draws something on that layer it should not. NEXT-ACTION: ~~count `(segment ...)` per layer in the file and compare with `trace_segment_count` per layer in the snapshot~~ - run, and the fixtures turned out to hold one copper segment between them. See V1 for what that means and what replaces this action.
- NEXT-ACTION: **wire the variant panel or delete it** - `auto_route_variants()` on one side, `showVariants()` on the other, and no line between them. Both are owner-facing calls: wiring it is autorouter work the owner has de-prioritised, deleting it throws away a scored-variant UI. Park it behind D5 rather than guessing.
- DONE: **the same audit on the TypeScript side, and it found a feature that is half built.** No viewer test lacks an assertion outright, but two in `reliability.spec.ts` wrapped every assertion in `if (await shareBtn.isVisible())` - and **`#share-btn` does not exist in index.html**, nor does anything in the viewer write a URL to the clipboard. Both tests passed having checked nothing, permanently, which is the shape that hid a dead keyboard shortcut for months. The reading half of that feature is real: the app decodes view state out of a URL on load and sets the layer checkboxes from it. That half is tested now - `?l=top` has to leave the top box checked and the other two clear - and producing such a URL is recorded here as missing rather than pretended.
