The writer emits each via as a block of its own, `trace GND { via 20mm,10mm drill 0.3mm }`, with no path in it. `sync_trace` decided whether to read geometry by testing `any(matches!(d, TraceDirective::Path(_)))`, so a block holding only a via never reached the branch that spawns one: route, save, reload, and every via was gone along with the layer change it carried. Silently. The predicate accepts a via now, and `crates/cypcb-world/tests/dsl_round_trip.rs` holds the line two ways - geometry in equals geometry out, and writing twice produces the same bytes, because a format whose output depends on how many times it has been through the tool is not a storage format.
- DONE: **one fixture that uses the whole language, and eight tests that it arrives.** The via bug was not a parser bug - the grammar read the via, the AST held it, and sync dropped it - and nothing caught that because no test asked whether what a file says is what the model ends up holding. `crates/cypcb-world/tests/language_conformance.rs` asks, for every construct there is: board and outline, an inline footprint with pads and silkscreen, components with placement, rotation, side and a typed value, net constraints and the class that fills in what a net leaves unsaid, a copper pour with its net beside a keepout without one, a pin-to-pin trace, a geometric one, a via block on its own, a placed and rotated module instance whose pin reaches the design's net, and the assertions. All eight passed first run. A construct added to the grammar without a line here is one nobody has checked reaches the board.
- **`examples/syntax.cypcb` is 28 lines and covers board, component, net and trace.** It is named as though it were the language reference and is a fifth of it. Left alone rather than grown, because the conformance test is the executable version and an example should read like a board someone would draw.
- DONE: **`setup-linux.sh` had a build host's own directory in it, and its last step could not do the one thing it was for.** Step 6 read `cd /workspace/codeyourpcb` - the path this project happens to sit in on the machine it is developed on. Under `set -e`, on anyone else's box, that line ends the script one step before the summary telling the reader what to run next. It changes to its own directory at the top now, and the whole script was run from `/tmp` to prove it: node, rust and the GTK check all found, stopping at `sudo: a password is required`, which is the container's limit rather than the script's.
- **The same step held a branch that could never run.** It called `cargo check -p cypcb-desktop` and then tested `if [ $? -eq 0 ]`, so with `set -e` a failing check exited the shell before the test - and `[WARN] Compilation check failed - may need additional dependencies` never printed, on exactly the machine that needed to read it. Demonstrated on its own: `set -e; false; if [ $? -eq 0 ]; then ...; fi; echo AFTER` prints nothing and exits 1. With the command in the condition, the real failing check now prints the warning and reaches the end: `[WARN] Compilation check failed`, `REACHED_END`, `exit=0`.
- `viewer/src/__tests__/the-setup-scripts-run-on-a-strangers-machine.test.ts` holds all nine setup/dev/build scripts to both rules. Mutation-checked on the previous commit: three of its sixteen tests fail. **Its first version failed on the fixed tree too** - it read the comment explaining the bug and matched the `$?` inside it, so it now strips comments before scanning: a test that cannot tell code from prose about code.
- DONE: **`DESKTOP-SETUP.md` named four installer files that will never exist.** It gives the reader exact paths - `CodeYourPCB_0.1.0_x64_en-US.msi`, `code-your-pcb_0.1.0_amd64.AppImage` and two more - while `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` says `"version": "0.1.0-beta"`, which is the string Tauri builds the filename from. Every path was off by the suffix. They are replaced by the directories, plus the sentence that says where the name comes from: a document that repeats a version goes stale on the next release, and the build scripts already print what they produced.
- **Two size claims went with them.** "Instalator ~5-10MB", "Disk image ~8-12MB" and "mały (5-10MB)" are numbers nobody has measured - no bundle has ever been built from this tree - so they are gone rather than carried. What is checkable was checked and is true: `F11` for fullscreen and `Ctrl+Shift+T` for the theme are the shortcuts `menu.rs` binds, all nine setup/dev/build scripts the page names exist, and the menu bar really does have File, Edit, View and Help.
- **The first version of that guard passed against the very document it was written for.** `\b\d+\.\d+\.\d+\b` finds nothing in `CodeYourPCB_0.1.0_x64` because `_` is a word character, so the test reported green on the broken doc. Without the boundaries it fails on the previous commit with `the page names 0.1.0, tauri.conf.json says 0.1.0-beta`. A guard has to be run against the fault before it is trusted.
- DONE: **the desktop build had never worked from the scripts that build it, and they reported success anyway.** Running `./build-linux.sh` in a fresh checkout: `Couldn't recognize the current folder as a Tauri project. It must contain a tauri.conf.json ... in any subfolder`, then `Build complete!`, then `AppImage (portable): (not created)`, then `SCRIPT_EXIT=0`. Two faults in one run, neither reachable by compiling anything.
- **The first is a path.** The Tauri CLI looks for `tauri.conf.json` in the current folder or below it, and `src-tauri` is a **sibling** of `viewer`, not a child - so `npm run build:desktop`, which runs with `viewer` as its directory, could never find the project on any machine, with or without the system libraries. `dev:desktop` and `build:desktop` change to the repo root first now. Proven: from the root the CLI gets as far as `Running beforeBuildCommand`, and `--config ../src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` from `viewer` does **not** help - project detection reads the directory, not the flag.
- **The second is a lie.** `build-linux.sh`, `build-macos.sh` and `build-windows.bat` each ran the build, ignored its exit code and printed `Build complete!`. They check it now and say `Build FAILED` with what to try. Re-run after both fixes, the same command fails on the real reason - `The pkg-config command could not be found` - and exits **1**, with the advice pointing at `setup-linux.sh`, which does install `pkg-config` and the GTK set the README names.
- `viewer/src/__tests__/the-desktop-scripts-do-what-they-print.test.ts` holds both: the npm scripts have to leave `viewer` before running Tauri, and every build script has to name a failure before it can claim a success. Mutation-checked on the previous commit: **six of its seven tests fail**.
- DONE: **the desktop app shipped an Edit menu where five items did nothing.** `src-tauri` is excluded from every test run - it needs system GTK and webkit, and `cargo check -p cypcb-desktop` in this container stops at `The pkg-config command could not be found`, so the exclusion is honest and the crate is read by nothing. Reading it as text found the gap: the native menu declares an id per item, `menu.rs` acts on `file.quit` and `view.fullscreen` itself and emits the rest as `menu-action`, and `desktop.ts` switches on nine of them. **`edit.undo`, `edit.redo`, `edit.cut`, `edit.copy` and `edit.paste` fell through to `default:` and wrote a console line.** Undo and redo are real features whose keyboard shortcut has always worked, so the menu entry did nothing while `Ctrl+Z` did - the kind of gap only somebody running the desktop build would find.
- Undo and redo are wired to the app's own undo stack. Cut, copy and paste are **removed from the menu** rather than routed somewhere: they belong to whatever has focus, the code editor and every input already bind them, and a clipboard command aimed at nothing is worse than no menu entry.
- **Wiring them turned up a third copy of the same body and a drift between the first two.** Undo was written out at the toolbar button and again in the keyboard handler, and the toolbar copy forgot `interactionState.selectedTraceId` - so undoing from the button left a trace selected for dragging in the interaction layer after the entity it named was gone. One `performUndo` and one `performRedo` now, called from the toolbar, the keyboard and the desktop menu.
- `viewer/src/__tests__/every-native-menu-item-does-something.test.ts` compares the ids `menu.rs` declares against the handlers on both sides, in both directions, without a compiler - which is the only kind of check this crate can have here. Mutation-checked on the previous commit: `menu entries that emit an event nobody handles: edit.undo, edit.redo, edit.cut, edit.copy, edit.paste`.
- DONE: **`interface` is a contract now, not a comment.** It was the last v2 construct that parsed and was read by nothing: `examples/v2-interfaces.cypcb` declared I2C, SPI, Power and UART, and two modules whose comments said "// I2C pins" and "// Power pins" - a claim no command could check and no user could rely on. A module signs an interface with `implements I2C`, written once per interface the way `pin` is written once per pin, and the checker holds it to every pin that interface declares.
- Two errors, both reported with the line that caused them and both exiting 1: claiming an interface nobody defined (`module 'Sensor' implements 'SPI', which is not defined. Defined: I2C, Power`) and claiming one without its pins. The second reads, on a real file:

```
cypcb::sync::interface_not_satisfied
  x module 'Sensor' implements 'I2C' without pin SDA
   ,-[8:11]
 8 | interface I2C {
   :           -+-
   :            `-- declared here
14 |     implements I2C
   :     ------+-------
   :           `-- this promises SDA
   `----
  help: add `pin SDA` to the module, or drop the claim
```

- **Checked over the definitions rather than over the instances**, because a module nobody instantiates is exactly the case this catches: a library file of blocks has no board and no `use`, and its promises still have to hold. `SyncError` is the right home - it already carries `UnknownModule` and `UnconnectedModulePin`, so a broken contract is refused where every other semantic error is, in every command that loads a board.
- The construct had to be taught to **both** parsers - `grammar.js` plus a regenerated `parser.c`, and `reader.rs`, the hand-written one this project ships - and `tests/differential.rs` is what proves they agree: it compares the two ASTs of every example, and the example now uses `implements`.
- Four tests in `cypcb-world`, mutation-checked: with `check_interface_contracts` returning early, `a_module_claiming_an_interface_without_its_pins_is_reported` and `a_module_claiming_an_interface_nobody_defined_is_reported` both fail. The satisfied case and the module that claims nothing pass either way, which is the point of having them: a module written before `implements` existed must keep working.
- `language_conformance.rs` gained the construct, per its own rule that a feature added to the grammar without a line there is one nobody has checked reaches the board. `docs/SYNTAX.md` gained a **Modules and Interfaces** section - the guide documented none of the v2 constructs, and the two that are now fully enforced are written up with the errors they produce; `the_syntax_guide_parses` reads them, once `interface` was added to its list of top-level keywords.
- **The language server offered a completion the language has never accepted.** Inside a component body it suggested `pin.${1:1} = ${2:NET}` - the same `pin.<N> = <NET>` shape that was removed from `docs/SYNTAX.md` as never having worked - so an editor user could accept it and get nothing. Removed, and a `goto.rs` fixture that was written in it now uses a trace. Module and interface bodies get real completions instead: `pin`, `implements`, `component`, `net`, `use`.
- DONE: **`pin.1 = VCC` is a connection now.** Chasing the silently-dropped-property finding turned up something worse than a typo being ignored: the shorthand for naming a component's nets in place parsed on both sides, rode the AST, was read by the language server for hover and go-to-definition - and the board model never looked at `ComponentDef::net_assignments`. Measured on a two-resistor board wired entirely that way: `cypcb parse` reported `"pins": []` for both parts and `cypcb check` gave **four `unconnected-pin` violations** for a file that names every connection it has. After: two nets, and the only complaint left is that no copper reaches them yet.
- **This also corrects the V6 entry below.** It recorded `pin.<N> = <NET>` as a shape "the parser has never accepted" and removed it from `docs/SYNTAX.md`; the truth is that the parser always accepted it and the world crate discarded it. The guide documents it again, with the measurement and a line saying what used to happen, and the language server offers it again.
- Folded in `sync_ast_to_world` rather than wired into the component pass, so nets keep one path: pin validation against the footprint, constraints, classes and the ratsnest all run over `net` blocks, and an assignment is that statement written at the other end. The folded blocks are **appended**, never merged into the block sharing their name - a net is synchronised in place and reaching for a component not yet synchronised is `UnknownComponent`, so merging into a `net` block written above the parts reported the design's own components as undefined. Two blocks naming one net are already one net; the registry interns by name.
- A net named by both forms keeps the block's constraints: `net SIG [width 0.5mm] { R1.2 }` beside `pin.1 = SIG` on another part is one 0.5mm net, and `assert SIG.width >= 0.5mm` is silent on it.
- DONE: **a word a block does not have is an error now.** Every block body in the Rust reader ended in `_ => { self.bump(); }`, so `rotat 90` in a component - one letter short of `rotate` - was read, dropped and never mentioned: the part came out unrotated and `cypcb check` called the board fine. Measured against tree-sitter, which reports all of these: eight fixtures, one per block, and the Rust reader **accepted six of them** - board, component, trace, footprint, zone and module. Only `net` and `interface` already complained.
- The reader reports them now, one error per line rather than per token, naming the word and what the block does take:

```
cypcb::parse::unknown_property
  x `component` has no property `zzz`
 6 |   zzz 5
   :   -+-
   :    `-- not a property of `component`
  help: `component` takes: value, at, rotate, pin.<N> = <NET>
```

- **`stackup` had to be implemented first.** A board block that refuses what it does not recognise would have refused a real construct: `stackup { copper 0.035mm prepreg 0.2mm ... }` is in the grammar, tree-sitter has always read it into `BoardDef::stackup`, and this reader fell into the same silent arm that swallowed typos. It reads it now, and `both_readers_read_the_same_stackup` compares the two ASTs layer by layer - `differential.rs` could not have caught this, because it only reads `examples/` and no example declares one. **Nothing downstream consumes `BoardDef::stackup`**: the checker takes its stackup from the fab preset, so this is the third construct in this language that parses and is read by nobody. Recorded rather than fixed - what a design's own stackup should override is a fab question, not a parser one.
- All thirteen fixtures now fail on both readers and every example still passes: `cargo test -p cypcb-parser --features rust-parser,tree-sitter-parser` -> 5 parity, 3 differential, 98 unit, 0 failed, and a scan of all 20 examples through `cypcb check` reports no `unknown_property`.
- DONE: **the editor was blind to every semantic error, and could not follow an import.** Two findings, one root: `DocumentState::new(_uri, ...)` threw the document's own URI away with a comment saying it was "used for debugging but not stored", and `run_diagnostics` published parse errors and DRC violations only. The sync's errors were collected and dropped.
- Measured through the protocol, by driving the binary the way an editor does. A board whose component names a footprint that does not exist: **zero diagnostics**, while `cypcb check` on the same text exits 1. After: `unknown footprint: 'NOSUCHFOOTPRINT'`, on the line that wrote it. Everything `SyncError` reports arrives now - duplicate refdes, a net naming a pin the part does not have, a module pin left unconnected, a broken interface contract - in the same words the command line prints, because the conversion reads miette's `Diagnostic` off the error rather than re-wording each variant.
- **`import` was never the construct that does nothing.** The previous fire recorded it as the last one; it is resolved by all five CLI commands through `cypcb_parser::resolve_imports`. What had no resolution was the editor: `examples/v2-imports.cypcb` came up as an empty board there while the same file reports ten unrouted pins on the command line. The document knows its path now and resolves imports exactly as the CLI does, against its own directory. The resolved AST builds the model and is never stored - hover, go-to-definition and completion read the document's own AST, whose spans point into its own text, and an imported definition's span points into another file. A span past the end of this document is reported at its start rather than at a position invented for it.
- An import that cannot be resolved is a diagnostic on the `import` statement that wrote it, found by matching `ImportError::path()` against the AST rather than defaulting to the first character of the file.
- Both mutation-checked: with the sync-error loop emptied, `a_semantic_error_reaches_the_editor` fails with `the footprint does not exist and the editor said nothing about it: []`; with `path_of` never matching `file://`, `a_design_that_imports_its_blocks_is_understood` fails with `the imported blocks were never instantiated, so the editor is checking an empty board: []`. `cargo test -p cypcb-lsp --test the_language_server_answers` -> 11 passed.
- DONE: **the engine can follow an import without a filesystem, and a selective import stopped losing what it needs.** A browser tab has no disk, so `cypcb-render` could not open `lib/blocks.cypcb` and a design split across files loaded as `unknown module` for every block it imports - while the same file reports ten unrouted pins on the command line. The resolution was never the problem; where the bytes come from was. `resolve_imports` is now one algorithm over an `ImportSource`: `FromDisk` for the CLI and the language server, `FromMemory` for a host that has already fetched the files. `PcbEngine::load_source_with_imports(source, files_json)` takes `{"lib/blocks.cypcb": "..."}` and resolves relative paths, cycles, selective imports and libraries built from libraries exactly as the disk version does.
- **Writing the test for a library built from libraries found a real language bug.** `import Dot from "lib/dot.cypcb"` took the module and nothing else, so a block whose parts use a footprint that same file declares arrived without it: `unknown footprint: 'TINY'` on a design that never named a footprint. A library that cannot be used without knowing what is inside it is not a library. A selective import now also takes what the named definitions depend on - the footprints their components use, the interfaces they implement, the modules they instantiate, transitively - and says nothing about them, because the design never asked for them by name. Mutation-checked: with the closure disabled, `a_library_may_be_built_from_libraries` fails with exactly that message.
- `load_source` without host files now reports `cannot read import 'lib/blocks.cypcb' ... the host supplied no files` rather than `unknown module`, which points at the host rather than at the design.
- Five tests in `crates/cypcb-render/tests/the_browser_can_follow_an_import.rs`: the blocks arrive and bring their parts, a file the host did not supply names the path and what was on offer, a design with no imports is unchanged, a library imports a library, and a host that sends rubbish instead of JSON is told what was expected.
- DONE: **a design split across files opens in the browser, from the project manager, like any other.** The engine could be handed files and nothing handed it any: `main.ts` called `load_source` on twelve paths. All twelve go through one `loadDesign` now, which passes whatever of the library is in hand; `followImports` fetches what the design names, follows imports of imports, and loads again when something new arrives - the same shape as `reloadAfterLcscFetch`, which already did this for footprints fetched from LCSC.
- Where the files come from depends on how the design arrived, and the viewer says so rather than guessing: a template is served over HTTP so its library is a fetch beside it (`/templates/lib/blocks.cypcb`); a recent file is text out of localStorage with no home, so there is nowhere to fetch from and the engine's own message stands - it names the path it wanted and what the host supplied.
- `viewer/src/imports.ts` is the host's half: which paths a file imports (both forms, ignoring commented-out ones), and `normalisePath` mirroring `normalise` in `crates/cypcb-parser/src/imports.rs` so the two agree on which key a file is stored under. Nine unit tests, including the one that matters for a graph rather than a tree: two libraries importing the same third ask for it once.
- **The feature is reachable by a user, not only by a test.** `viewer/public/templates/sensor-front-end.cypcb` imports `lib/blocks.cypcb` and places three instances of what it finds there; it is the fifth template card. `cypcb check` on it: 12 unrouted pins, no unconnected ones, rc=0 - which is what an unrouted board should say. Writing it found a bug in its own library: `Divider` had no `GND` pin, so both dividers left `RBOT.2` on no net at all.
- `viewer/e2e/a-template-brings-its-library.spec.ts` clicks the card and waits for the board the template describes: `DIV_A_RTOP`, `DIV_B_RTOP` and `STATUS_D1` are all parts of imported modules, and nothing in the design names a resistor. Mutation-checked: with `importReader` left null on the template path, it fails.
- **Two lists of templates, and the gate pinned the wrong number.** The cards live in `index.html` and the files in `project-manager.ts`, so adding one meant editing both - and `project-manager.spec.ts` asserted `toHaveCount(5)` and `templateCount === 4`, numbers that had to be edited to add a template. Both now count against the registry, and `every-template-card-is-a-template` fails if the two lists disagree or a card names a file that is not there.
- DONE: **the dev server hands a design its library - and stopped handing a web page the whole disk.** Wiring `read-file` meant reading the two handlers next to it, and both were wide open. `server.ts` speaks WebSocket on localhost with no authentication and no origin check, so anything it will do, any page in the browser can ask it to do - and it read whatever path a message named (`readFileSync(message.file)` in `open-file`) and wrote whatever a message named (`writeFileSync(message.file, message.content)` in `save`). A page could have read a developer's keys or written over their shell profile. Every path a client names now goes through `insideWatchDir`: relative paths resolve against the watched directory, and anything that climbs out of it with `..` or an absolute path is refused.
- `read-file` answers with the text beside the design, which is the last of the three ways a design reaches the viewer. A workspace file arrives over the same socket, so `importReader` becomes a round trip rather than a fetch, and the paths it asks for are joined to the design's own directory.
- `viewer/src/__tests__/the-dev-server-guards-the-disk.test.ts` spawns the real server on a temporary workspace and speaks the real protocol to it: it hands over a file inside the directory, refuses to read one outside, refuses to open one outside, refuses to write outside, and still saves inside. Mutation-checked: with the containment removed, the three refusals fail and the two positive cases pass.
- **The first mutation run said the opposite, and that was the test lying.** It spawned the server on the pinned port 4322, which a developer's own dev server already held, so the new process bound nothing and the probe connected to theirs - a green test measuring a different program. The port is `CYPCB_WS_PORT` now and the test takes a free one; `CYPCB_NO_VITE=1` runs the socket half without starting a second Vite on Playwright's port.
- DONE: **the rest of the dev server's front door.** Fixing `open-file` and `save` left two holes of the same shape open. `route` took the path from the message and never checked it - the CLI ran with that path as its argument and its directory as the working directory, and the handler then read `<file>.ses` and `<file>.routes` beside it and sent them back, so an unchecked path there was both a write and a read of anywhere on the disk. It goes through `insideWatchDir` now.
- **And the socket accepted connections from any page on the internet.** WebSocket is not subject to CORS: a page a developer visits while the dev server runs can open `ws://localhost:4322` and start naming files. Containment bounds what it could reach, but it could still overwrite the boards in the watched directory. A browser always sends `Origin` and cannot be talked out of it, so a page served by a different host is refused with close code 1008.
- **The rule is same-host, not localhost**, because a board is often looked at from another machine on the LAN and that browser's origin is this machine's network address. A test pins exactly that: `Origin: http://192.168.0.10:7001` with a matching `Host` connects, `https://not-this-project.example` is closed. A localhost-only rule would have refused the browser actually in use.
- `save` also refused an empty file: `!message.content` is true of `''`, so clearing a board and saving it did nothing and answered "Missing file path or content".
- Ten tests over the real server and the real protocol, each mutation-checked - with the route guard removed, `refuses to route a file outside it` fails; with the origin check removed, `refuses a browser page it does not serve` fails.
- DONE: **`stackup` is consumed now - the last construct in the language that parsed and was read by nobody.** Both parsers have read `stackup { copper 0.035mm core 1.5mm copper 0.035mm }` into `BoardDef::stackup` since the board block started refusing what it does not recognise, and this file recorded on 2026-08-07 that nothing downstream consumed it. It reaches the world as a `Stackup` component and the checker reads it, which closes the list this project has been working through: `interface`, `pin.N`, `lcsc`, `diffpair`, and now this.
- **Two contradictions, both answerable from the design alone.** A stackup describing a different number of copper layers than `layers N` does - the exporter takes its Gerber count from `layers`, so the files and the build instructions would describe different boards - and two copper layers pressed together with no dielectric between them, which is one thicker foil and a short by construction. `mask` and `silk` do not separate copper, and when one of them is what a designer put between two copper layers the message says so rather than only that something is wrong.
- **What it deliberately does not check is the thickness.** The stackup's total is a number this tool can compute and has no table to judge: what a fab will press is fab data this project does not have, and a range invented here would be a fabrication decision made by a guess. The total goes in the message - `1.570mm of material` - where somebody who does know can read it. A stackup where any layer omits its thickness reports no total at all, because a partial sum reads like a measurement.
- The registry is 21 rules - `grep -c 'Box::new(rules::' crates/cypcb-drc/src/lib.rs` - and no existing board changes verdict: no example in this repository declares a stackup, so the rule is silent on all of them.
- Proof: `cargo test -p cypcb-drc --test the_stackup_has_to_match_the_board` -> 9 passed (the rule, including the two controls: a matching stackup and no stackup at all); `cargo test -p cypcb-cli --test a_stackup_that_lies_is_reported` -> 4 passed, which drives the same fault from a file through both parsers, `sync_ast_to_world` and the command a person runs.
- **The control caught its own assertion first.** `a_stackup_that_agrees_with_the_board_is_silent` looked for the word `stackup` anywhere in the output and failed on a correct board - the temporary file's path contained it. It matches the violation line now. A control that fails for the wrong reason is not a control.
- **The panel guard written last fire earned itself on a real change rather than a mutation.** Adding the kind and running the viewer suite: `these arrive in the panel as a raw slug: stackup`, 1 failed of 5, before a line of TypeScript was touched. That is the drift it exists to catch, caught the first time it happened.
- `docs/SYNTAX.md` documents the construct for the first time - the layer types, that thickness is optional, what the checker reports and what it refuses to judge. The guide's own parse test holds the example to the grammar.
- NEXT-ACTION: none pulled here. The dev server's whole message surface - `open-file`, `read-file`, `save`, `route`, `list-files` - is checked, and every path a client names is resolved inside the watched directory.
- QUEUED: DSL v2's remaining construct is `import` - the path is parsed and nothing resolves it.
- DONE: **a design written here can be opened in KiCad.** The importer has read `.kicad_pcb` for months and `writer.rs` could put routed copper back into a file it came from, but nothing turned a `.cypcb` design into a KiCad board - so a design written in this language could be checked, routed and turned into fabrication files, and still could not be opened by anybody who does not use this tool. `cypcb to-kicad <file>` writes one: the outline on `Edge.Cuts`, a footprint per part with its pads and their nets, every trace as a `(segment ...)`, every via as a `(via ...)`, and the net list they refer to.
- **Not written, and said so rather than guessed:** zones, silkscreen text, 3D models, the setup block's dozens of fields. KiCad fills its own defaults for what a file leaves out; inventing values for them here would be inventing a board.
- Verified the only way it can be without KiCad in the room - by this project's own importer, which reads the benchmark boards. `examples/blink.cypcb` out and back: 9 components, 7 nets, 60x40mm, matching the design. `examples/four-layer.cypcb`: 6 components, 3 nets, 2 trace segments. Four tests in `crates/cypcb-cli/tests/a_design_opens_in_kicad.rs` cover the round trip, a rotated part keeping its angle, every pad naming its net, and an empty board still being a board. The claim is "the importer reads it", not "pcbnew opens it", and the file says so.
- Mutation-checked: with the footprint's rotation written as 0, `a_rotated_part_keeps_its_angle` fails - which is the defect this project already had once in the other direction, in the importer's origin handling.
- ~~QUEUED: copper pour / ground planes - the claim that no `Zone` type exists was wrong; the gap is that a zone cannot name its net~~ **Stale on both counts.** `examples/pour-island.cypcb` has `zone gnd_pour { bounds ... layer top net GND }`, and the conformance fixture has covered a pour with its net beside a keepout without one since the language-conformance work. Nothing to do here.
- DONE: **differential pairs are a thing the language says and the checker measures.** `diffpair USB { USB_DP USB_DM }` declares two nets that carry one signal, and `length_match_tolerance` - a field every fab preset has carried and nothing has ever read - is what the skew between them is measured against. A pair routed 10mm apart on JLCPCB reports `diff-pair-skew: diffpair 'USB': USB_DP runs 30.000mm and USB_DM runs 40.000mm`, with 0.5mm required; the same board with both halves straight is silent.
- **A pair naming a net the board does not have is reported too**, because a typo there would otherwise turn the whole check off without a word - the same failure the interface contracts were built to avoid.
- Not measured, and the guide says so: the gap between the two halves. That is the other half of a differential-pair rule and it needs the router to place them alongside each other first.
- The construct went through both parsers, the AST, sync, the world, a DRC rule and the syntax guide, which `the_guide_shows_every_construct` now demands. Three end-to-end tests through the binary; mutation-checked with the skew comparison disabled, which fails `a_pair_routed_the_long_way_round_is_reported`.
- **The last hand-written preset table is gone with it.** `DesignRules::prototype()` still carried thirteen numbers by hand; adding a fourteenth field would have meant inventing one, so it delegates to the shared table like every other preset - which `prototype_kept_every_number_it_had` already proves is the same board.
- DONE: **followed a declared outline through the whole chain, and one end of it was writing the wrong board.** `outline { point ... }` is in the language and no example used it, so what actually honours it had never been measured. On an L-shaped board: the checker measures edge clearance against the real edge and reports a part in the cut-away (`R1: Edge clearance violation: 0.00mm actual, 0.30mm required`); the Gerber exporter writes all six segments to `Edge_Cuts.gbr`; **`to-kicad` wrote the four corners of the `size` rectangle**, so the file a KiCad user opened was a different board from the one the fab got. It writes the shape the design states now - eight lines for the new `examples/cutout.cypcb`, including the slot's own walls.
- **The router ignores the outline, and the checker catches it.** Measured on a U-shaped board with a part in each arm, both well inside the copper: the built-in router lays `path 4.445mm,25.019mm -> 34.417mm,25.019mm`, straight across the slot, and `cypcb check` on the result reports `edge-clearance at (20.574mm, 25.019mm): trace 'SIG'`. So the tool cannot ship that board silently - but it does route it, and the router is V2, which the owner has de-prioritised. Recorded with the fixture rather than fixed.
- `examples/cutout.cypcb` is the language made visible: a U-shaped board with parts in each arm, clean apart from being unrouted, and a comment telling the reader to move a part into the slot and watch the checker say so.
- DONE: **the screen draws the board's real shape.** The same defect the KiCad writer had, on the surface a person actually looks at: `drawBoardOutline` took `width_nm` and `height_nm` and drew a rectangle, whatever shape the design stated - so a board with a slot or a cutout looked like a plain rectangle here while the checker measured against the real edge and the Gerbers were cut to it. The outline reaches the snapshot now (`BoardInfo::outline`, absent when the design states none) and the renderer follows it point by point.
- Four tests in `viewer/src/__tests__/the-board-is-drawn-as-the-shape-it-is.test.ts` over a recording canvas: the path follows every corner including the slot's inner one at 25mm,10mm, no rectangle is drawn when there is an outline, the rectangle is still drawn when there is not, and an outline too short to be a shape falls back rather than drawing a line. Mutation-checked: with the outline branch disabled the first two fail.
- DONE: **the design can name the part to buy, and the bill of materials carries it.** The viewer has fetched footprints from LCSC for a long time and the way a design asked for one was `lcsc "C7593"` inside a component - which the grammar did not have. The reader dropped it in silence, so the browser read it out of the raw source text with a regular expression while the board model never saw it, and when unknown properties became an error two fires ago **the same file stopped checking at all**: `component has no property 'lcsc'`. That is a regression this project caused, found by measuring the parts engine rather than by anybody reporting it.
- It is a property of the language now, through both parsers, the AST, the ECS and the exporter. `cypcb export` writes JLCPCB's own `LCSC Part #` column, empty for a component that names no part - which is what their assembly form expects. The part number is part of the grouping key, so two 10k 0402s from different catalogue entries are two lines to order rather than one line of twice as many of the wrong thing.
- Measured end to end: `lcsc "C7593"` on an NE555 gives `U1,SOIC-8,1,NE555,C7593` in `parts-BOM.csv`. Three CLI tests, mutation-checked - with the part dropped from the grouping key, two of the three fail.
- DONE: **the browser stopped reading the language with a regular expression.** With `lcsc` a real property, the part number rides the snapshot, so `autoFetchLcscFootprints` reads the board model instead of `/component\s+\w+\s+\w+\s+"([^"]+)"\s*\{[^}]*lcsc\s+"C(\d+)"[^}]*\}/` over the raw source. That expression was a second reader of the language - what `docs/one-parser.md` exists to prevent - and it was wrong in three ways nobody had measured: **it could not see a part inside a module** (the pattern needs a component at the top level), it missed any part number not spelled `C` then digits, and a component block containing a nested brace ended its match early.
- Three tests in `crates/cypcb-render/tests/the_snapshot_names_the_part_to_buy.rs`, including the module case the old expression could not reach: `S1_U2` arrives with `C84291`. A component naming no part has no `lcsc` field at all rather than an empty string, because "" is not an answer to "did the design name a part". Mutation-checked: with the field dropped from the snapshot, two of the three fail.
- Audited the rest of the viewer for the same shape. What is left is text handling that is honestly about text: `findComponentInsertLine` picks the line to write a new component at, and the LSP bridge reads a refdes off the line under the cursor. Neither reads meaning the model already holds.
- QUEUED: schematic generation, and the router's turn at the outline

### V6 - Documentation truth
- DONE: **a working shortcut that no user could find.** `CLAUDE.md` calls itself the canonical shortcut registry and asks that every new one be added to it and to the help modal. `Ctrl+L` - simplify the selected trace - reached neither, so it has worked for as long as its handler has existed and only its author could use it. Listed in both now, and a vitest case reads the keys the handlers actually compare against and requires the registry to know each: it reports `keys the code handles and CLAUDE.md does not list: l` when the entry is taken away. A registry that is only as good as the last person's memory is not a registry.
- DONE: **the syntax guide taught a component property the parser has never accepted.** `docs/SYNTAX.md` documented `pin.<N> = <NET>` as an optional way to name a component's nets, with a worked example - `pin.1 = VCC` - and the grammar has no such rule: connections are declared the other way round, in a `net` block. A reader who typed the example got a syntax error and no way to tell whether they had mistyped it or the guide was wrong. Removed, with a line saying it never worked so nobody restores it from memory. The new test parses every concrete example in the guide as a document - 10 of 34 blocks; the rest are templates written with `<placeholders>`, bodies elided as `{ ... }`, or blocks the guide deliberately shows as invalid, and each skipped block is named in the output rather than quietly passed over. It fails on the previous commit.
- **That entry was wrong about the cause, and the correction is below in V1.** `pin.<N> = <NET>` is a rule in `grammar.js` (`net_assignment`, line 174), an arm in `reader.rs`, a field on `ComponentDef` and something the language server reads. Both parsers accept it. What no reader did was *check*: the claim "the grammar has no such rule" was written from the absence of an effect, and the effect was missing further downstream.
- DONE: **`docs/architecture.md` was missing the largest crate in the workspace.** Thirteen crate entries each carried a `**Size**: ~N lines` snapshot and every one had drifted - `cypcb-drc` said 1,800 against a measured 7,221 - so they are replaced by the command that answers the question. Worse than stale numbers: **`cypcb-autoroute` (9,700 lines) and `cypcb-rules` (3,133) had no entry at all**, which is the router this project has spent most of its measurement on and the preset table every command now resolves `--preset` through. Both are written up, with dependency lists checked against their `Cargo.toml`. A diff of the doc's sections against `crates/*/` is now empty.
- DONE: **read `README.md` against the code, and four of its claims were out of date.** The feature table called silkscreen clearance a stub that runs in the viewer's JavaScript; it is a Rust rule and has been since the stubs were cleared. The benchmark table said 0 / 137 / 64 against a measured 2 / 271 / 210, from before the checker stopped merging faults, started measuring copper rather than part bodies, and began seeing footprints on imported boards - so the table now shows violations before routing, after, and how many the router introduced, with a line saying why the numbers went up while the boards did not get worse. The CLI section showed two commands of six and no flags; it shows all six, and that `check`, `route`, `score` and `export` share `--preset` and agree on the same board. The status paragraph named copper pour and a hidden button as the gaps and now names silkscreen text too, and says what the end-to-end chain actually checks. Every command block in it was run as written: `route --variants` then `score` on the file it produced reports 3 both ways.
